<note>The imagery here is rich in <term>Rosicrucianism|A secret philosophical society of the 17th century that used alchemical symbols to describe spiritual transformation</term> symbolism. The <term>Ouroboros|A snake eating its own tail</term> represents eternity and the cyclical nature of the universe. The <term>Pelican in its piety|A mother bird feeding her chicks with her own blood</term> is a traditional symbol of self-sacrifice and the "nourishing" stage of the alchemical process. The cubic stone at the bottom represents the "Philosopher's Stone" or the perfected soul, while the roses signify the unfolding of spiritual beauty.</note>

<meta>This is the title page of a 17th-century scholarly work on Gnostic amulets. The text is quite faded, but it establishes the authorship of John Macarius and the editorial involvement of Jean Chiflet.</meta>

# JOHN MACARIUS
## CANON OF AIRE. <note>original: "Canonici Ariensis" — a canon is a member of a cathedral's clergy; Aire is a town in northern France.</note>
# ABRAXAS,
->OR<-
# APISTOPISTUS, <note>original: "Apistopistus" — a Greek-derived term likely meaning "distrusting faith" or "skeptical belief," referring to the nature of the Gnostic charms.</note>
->WHICH IS AN ANTIQUARIAN<-
## INQUIRY INTO BASILIDIAN GEMS <note>Basilidians were a Gnostic sect from the 2nd century AD, known for creating intricate talismans and amulets.</note>
### DISCOURSE.
->TO WHICH IS ADDED<-
## PROTEAN ABRAXAS, <note>original: "Abraxas Proteus" — referring to Proteus, the Greek sea-god who could change his shape; here it implies the many forms the deity Abraxas takes on these gems.</note>
### OR THE MARVELOUS VARIETY OF THE
### MULTIFORM BASILIDIAN GEM.

<unclear>Preface by Jean Chiflet, Canon of Tournai, to John Macarius...</unclear> <note>Jean Chiflet (1612–1666) was a prominent scholar and physician who edited this work for publication after Macarius's death.</note>

->ANTWERP,<-
### FROM THE PLANTIN PRESS
### OF BALTHASAR MORETUS.

<summary>This title page introduces a scholarly investigation by John Macarius into "Abraxas" stones—mystical engraved gems used by the Basilidian Gnostics. Published posthumously in 1657 by the renowned Plantin Press, it remains a foundational text in the study of early Christian esotericism and archaeology.</summary>

<keywords>John Macarius, Abraxas, Gnosticism, Basilidians, Jean Chiflet, Plantin-Moretus Press, antiquarianism, amulets, Antwerp, 17th century scholarship</keywords>

# OF JEAN MACAIRE
## CANON OF AIRE <note>Aire-sur-la-Lys, a town in northern France.</note>
# **ABRAXAS,** <note>A mystical word found on ancient amulets, often used by Gnostic sects to represent a supreme deity or a numerical code for the 365 days of the year.</note>
->OR<-
# **THE UNBELIEVING-BELIEVER;** <note>original: "Apistopistus." This is a Greek-derived wordplay combining "apistos" (unbelieving) and "pistos" (faithful), likely referring to the paradoxical nature of these "magic" gems used by early heretical sects.</note>
### WHICH IS AN ANTIQUARIAN
### INQUIRY CONCERNING
### THE BASILIDIAN GEMS. <note>Basilidians were followers of Basilides, a 2nd-century Gnostic teacher in Alexandria. They were famous in the early modern period for the carved gemstones (amulets) they purportedly created.</note>
->**TO WHICH IS ADDED**<-
## **ABRAXAS PROTEUS,** <note>Proteus was a Greek sea god capable of changing his shape; the title suggests the "protean" or ever-changing varieties of these gems.</note>
### OR THE WONDROUS VARIETY OF THE 
### MULTIFORM BASILIDIAN GEM;

->Presented and illustrated with a commentary by **JEAN CHIFLET**, Canon of Tournai, and Preacher <note>original: "à Sacris Oratorij," referring here to a royal court orator or chaplain.</note> to the Catholic King **PHILIP IV** and the Most Serene Prince **JOHN OF AUSTRIA**.<-

<summary>The title page of a 1657 scholarly study on "Abraxas stones," ancient amulets associated with the Basilidian Gnostics. It marks a significant 17th-century effort to catalog and interpret archaeological finds through the lens of early church history and mysticism.</summary>

--- Page 9 ---
<meta>Continuity: This is the beginning of the dedication section, following the title page. The catchword "nibus" at the end of the page indicates the word "hominibus" (men) will be completed on the following page.</meta>

## TO THE MOST SERENE PRINCE
# J O H N
# O F  A U S T R I A,
## GOVERNOR-GENERAL OF THE BELGIANS AND THE SEQUANI,
<note>original: "Belgarum Sequanorumque Proregi." The Sequani were an ancient people of Gaul; in this period, the title refers to the Governor of the Franche-Comté and the Southern Netherlands under Spanish rule.</note>
# G O V E R N O R - G E N E R A L,
## BRAVE, PIOUS, [AND] FORTUNATE.

<image-desc>A large decorative woodcut initial 'A' entwined with floral vines and featuring two birds (possibly eagles) perched on the crossbars, facing away from each other.</image-desc>

WHILE Europe was armed against herself, and the world itself was at discord with itself, I attempted to track down the Sun amidst the shadows, **MOST SERENE PRINCE**. It was a joy, under the auspicious light of Your Fortune, and with Macarius <note>original: "vno Macario præeunte"; likely a reference to the scholar Jean Chiflet’s work on "Macarius" or a specific ancient source the author is using as a guide</note> alone leading the way, to enter the cave of Mithras <note>A Roman mystery religion centered on the god Mithras, often performed in underground "caves" or temples</note>, and from there to bring forth those monstrous idols which the first heretics <note>A reference to early Gnostic sects, whose engraved gems—often called Abraxas stones—are the subject of this study</note> held up in place of the Sun. Nothing is more wretched than these men—

<summary>A formal dedication to Don John of Austria, Governor-General of the Spanish Netherlands, framing the author's research into Gnostic antiquities as a journey through "Mithraic shadows" during a time of European war.</summary>
<keywords>Don John of Austria, dedication, Mithras, Gnosticism, heretics, Spanish Netherlands, Sequani, Abraxas</keywords>

--- Page 10 ---
<meta>Continuity: This page continues the dedication to Don John of Austria. The opening text completes the word "hominibus" (men) from the previous page's final sentence: "Nothing is more wretched than these men—"</meta>

—men, or more proud; who, while they seek the image and form of God <note>original: "effigiem Dei formámque"</note>, with a curious affectation for heavenly things, have branded the highest star with the shameful forms of countless animals and the impressions of all sorts of things, like so many spots. This cloudy and somber effort possessed something of serenity, and this work, though deformed by foul images, had this much good: that it sought the light. And nowhere is this light greater than in You.

Indeed, by Your arrival, You have cleared the clouds and dispersed the sadness of the Belgian sky <note>original: "cæli Belgici"; referring to the Spanish Netherlands</note>, which we suffered in our mourning when the Star departed for nine years <note>This likely refers to the departure of the previous Governor-General, Archduke Leopold Wilhelm, who served from 1647 to 1656</note>, had You not lent another light to the darkening Commonwealth <note>original: "Reipublicæ"</note>—illustrious, distinguished, seeing all things, and even hearing all things; in which one man, they say, the Sun pleased Homer, the prince of letters <note>A reference to the *Odyssey*, where the Sun is described as "he who sees all and hears all."</note>. Of the Spanish Em- <note>The text cuts off mid-word; the catchword for the next page is "pire" (Imperii).</note>

<summary>The author continues his flattering dedication to Don John of Austria, comparing the Prince’s arrival to the sun breaking through clouds after a nine-year period of gloom in the Spanish Netherlands.</summary>
<keywords>Don John of Austria, Belgian sky, Spanish Netherlands, Homer, sun imagery, political panegyric</keywords>

--- Page 11 ---
<meta>This page continues the celebratory address to a high-ranking leader, likely a member of the Habsburg or Spanish nobility, recounting his military and political successes across the Mediterranean before his arrival in Genoa.</meta>

<sig>* 3</sig>

You shine upon the people at intervals, appearing always greater and more brilliant to those upon whom you have most recently cast your light. With Sicily now pacified—which you governed with the wisdom of a mature man even in your tender years—with Naples restored, and with Populonia and Porto Longone <note>Porto Longone is the historical name for Porto Azzurro on the island of Elba</note> conquered, and Catalonia <note>original: "Gothalania"</note> recovered, you have counted as many triumphs as you have years. Having subdued the Tyrrhenian Sea, with the Turks and Moors opposing you in vain, the galley that carried You and your fortune, crowned with laurel, entered the longed-for harbors. Wherever you approached while hastening toward us, an equal joy was felt by all upon your arrival. In vain did you try to hide your fires: nowhere were you unrecognized; just like the Sun veiled by a thin mist and not wishing to be seen, they gazed up at you all the more greedily. Thus Genoa,

<summary>This page praises a military leader's victories in Sicily, Naples, Elba, and Catalonia, comparing his arrival in Genoa to the sun breaking through a mist.</summary>
<keywords>Sicily, Naples, Porto Longone, Catalonia, Tyrrhenian Sea, naval warfare, Turks, Moors, Genoa, panegyric</keywords>

--- Page 12 ---
<meta>The previous page likely introduced the addressee, a royal figure or military leader, as this page continues a panegyric regarding his arrival and early victories. The catchword "duce-" connects to the final word "introduceret" or similar on the following page.</meta>

Genoa, Milan, and even Venice <note>original: "Genua, Mediolanum, Venetiæ"</note> themselves were cheered by the gentler rays of Your Serenity. But oh, how happy are we, while we proclaim You happy! Belgium, leaning upon Your shoulders, has grown strong through Your youth and Your vigor. Scarcely had You arrived, sought after by the prayers of all—as if You were a second coming from PHILIP THE GREAT <note>referring to King Philip IV of Spain</note>, the Greatest of Kings—when You liberated our Tournai <note>original: "Tornacum," an ancient city in modern-day Belgium</note>, that ancient palace of the Franks, by opportunely sending in a garrison. Soon Valenciennes <note>original: "Valencenæ"</note> was besieged; but it was so that the laurel might wreathe Your brow, and that a victory gained over the enemies might decorate Your rising. So great is Your virtue, and so great Your good fortune, that opening a way for Yourself and Your men through impassable ramparts, You Yourself introduced the French into the city for which they so greatly sighed—

<summary>This page praises a royal military leader's arrival in the Low Countries, crediting him with the relief of Tournai and a victory at the siege of Valenciennes. It frames his military success as a divine or fated extension of the power of Philip the Great.</summary>
<keywords>panegyric, Tournai, Valenciennes, Philip IV of Spain, military victory, Belgium, Habsburg Netherlands</keywords>

--- Page 13 ---
<meta>Continuity note: This page continues the account of military successes, following the catchword 'fectis' from the previous page to complete the word 'perfectâ' (completed).</meta>

you would lead, but as captives. How much admiration did the swift recovery of Condé <note>original: "Condatum"; modern Condé-sur-l'Escaut</note> stir in us, and how much in the enemy! In the shortest possible time, once the massive siege works were completed, and after one or two pieces of artillery <note>original: "tormentis bellicis"</note> were discharged against the "winged mill" <note>original: "alatam molam"; likely a specific fortified windmill or bastion</note>, you struck down those giant structures by your presence alone, so to speak. To build those very works, the French treasury had been poured out for an entire year, without any fear that their labor would be in vain. Then truly, if ever before, the protection of the Virgin Mother shone forth, she who perpetually patronizes your affairs; for on the 15th of August, the day on which she was assumed into heaven, she wished to see you also triumphing in a bloodless victory <note>original: "incruentâ victoriâ"</note>, with four thousand five hundred of the besieged making a pact with you to be accepted into surrender. As they were worn out by labor and wasting away,

<summary>This page describes the rapid and nearly bloodless capture of Condé-sur-l'Escaut, attributing the victory to the commander's presence and the divine favor of the Virgin Mary on her feast day.</summary>
<keywords>Condé-sur-l'Escaut, siege warfare, artillery, French treasury, Virgin Mary, Feast of the Assumption, military surrender</keywords>

--- Page 14 ---
<meta>Continuity established with the previous page regarding the military campaign; the catchword "rat)" likely refers to "ratio" or "ratificatio" in the following text.</meta>

After those who were exhausted and useless for carrying out the business were finished, the strongest of the enemy who remained after the received slaughter at Valenciennes <note>original: "Valencenensi"; referring to the Siege of Valenciennes in 1656 during the Franco-Spanish War</note> attacked the monastery of Saint Venantius <note>Saint-Venant</note>, but they were forced to sound the retreat. They were not worthy to defeat those who were fighting under Your auspices and for a better cause. Then, at the village of Sainte-Hélène, which was not strengthened by any ramparts at all but was made famous by the fate of Gassion <note>Jean de Gassion (1609–1647), a Marshal of France who died from wounds received at the siege of Lens</note>, they did not dare to await the arrival of You and Your men. So that they might flee with less burden, they burned their own fortifications with their own torches. Thus, heaven brought forth for You one laurel after another, so that You might be everywhere:

->The Savior, The Besieger, The Illustrious.<-
<note>original Greek: "Σωτήρ, Πολιορκήτης, Επιφανής" — these were traditional titles given to Hellenistic kings</note>

In this year, in the month of March (from which the Romans used to measure time, and which Romulus had dedicated to his own father)

<summary>This page describes the retreat of enemy forces following the Battle of Valenciennes, highlighting their failure at Saint-Venant and Sainte-Hélène before transitioning to a discussion of the Roman calendar.</summary>
<keywords>Valenciennes, Saint-Venant, Jean de Gassion, military victory, Roman calendar, March, Romulus</keywords>

was) when, with the new Sun, a **Martial ardor** <note>original: "Martius ardor"; a poetic reference to the god Mars, signifying a passion for war</note> seized You, and the soldiers grew warm with that eagerness of Yours: the **Hannonians** <note>likely referring to the people of Hainaut, a historical region in the Low Countries</note> were liberated from a heavy enemy: they saw the earth soaking with his blood before they saw it clothed in the first colors of Spring: **Gislonopolis** <note>The Latinized name for Ghislage or a similar fortified town in Hainaut</note>, a town of **Hainaut**, most strongly fortified by art and its marshy location, yielded to Your power. The fame of the victory grew by the very difficulty of the feat, while by open force, and not a long one, You took possession of those whom rumor boasted could only be conquered by starvation. Now, by my work, such as it is, **heretical envy** shall also acknowledge You as victor:

> *You shall bring back the memorable spoil of the viperous monster.* <note>A poetic allusion, likely comparing the heresy to the Hydra or a similar mythological beast.</note>

And now, poured out at Your august feet, lies the barbarity of the **Basilidians** <note>Followers of Basilides, a 2nd-century Gnostic teacher; here used as a rhetorical label for contemporary heretics</note>, nor—

--- Page 16 ---
<meta>Continuity note: This page continues the formal dedication to a high-ranking military leader, likely a prince or sovereign, following the catchword "das" from the previous page.</meta>

which dares to rise up to You, the conqueror of monsters on land and sea. Those men used to call themselves the Princes of the world; these you have defeated. In a barbarous and fierce ritual, they used to frequent <note>original: "cœnas Thyeftæas"</note> Thyestean feasts <note>A reference to the Greek myth of Thyestes, used here to signify extreme cruelty or cannibalistic savagery</note>; you have warred down that very ferocity. Crush these monsters as well, as a new <term>Alcides</term> <note>A traditional name for Hercules, the mythological hero known for his strength and for slaying monsters</note>. Your bravery has no need for these amulets, however precious they may be or how much they deceive with a wasteful reputation.

Through the image of the Virgin <term>Mother of God</term> <note>original: "Deiparæ"</note>, which you carry before you in war with the motto, <term>UNDER YOUR PROTECTION</term> <note>original: "SVB TVVM PRÆSIDIVM," a famous ancient Christian hymn to the Virgin Mary</note>, you make it publicly known under what auspices you attack the enemy, and by whose patronage you conquer. Success to you with this <term>Palladium</term> <note>original: "Palladio"; a sacred image or safeguard that protects a city or army</note>, **MOST SERENE PRINCE**; and, as you are doing, having returned from your camp station to winter rest, lay your laurels into the lap of the best of Mothers, so that there they may be converted into the olive branch of peace,

<summary>This dedication page praises a military leader as a modern Hercules (Alcides) who fights under the religious protection of the Virgin Mary. The author encourages the Prince to transition from the "laurels" of war to the "olive branch" of peace during his winter retreat.</summary>

--- Page 17 ---
<meta>This page continues the formal dedication to the Prince, following the initial salutation. The catchword "ABRA-" on the previous page likely refers to the beginning of the dedication's narrative or a specific historical reference.</meta>

<sig>** 2</sig>

from which you have long since drawn a harmony of all virtues, and a majesty of form so composed and so benign, that it is plainly uncertain whether your countenance instills itself more into our minds or our sight. By this quality you ensure—especially while I address you, **Most Serene Prince**, and before I approach the work I have undertaken—that I am inclined to charge those with vanity who say that gemstones are useful to those about to approach Kings, provided that eagles or scarab beetles <note>original: "scarabaei"; ancient amulets often carved with these symbols were thought to grant favor with royalty</note> are engraved upon them. The inner chambers of the Palace lie open to those approaching to pay you honor, nor do you begrudge access to yourself: whoever has approached remains by your side, and only modesty and reverence for your Serenity brings an end to the conversation. To your supreme virtue and greatest piety, I present this **Proteus** <note>original: "Protevm"; a reference to the shape-shifting sea god, likely used here as a metaphor for the multifaceted nature of the book's subject or the author's versatility</note>

<summary>The author praises the Prince's accessible and virtuous character, dismissing the need for ancient superstitious amulets when seeking an audience with such a gracious ruler.</summary>

--- Page 18 ---
<meta>This page serves as the formal dedication of the work, following the title page. The author, Jean Chiflet, establishes the purpose of his study on Gnostic gems.</meta>

I append the Abraxas <note>original: "Abraxam"; a mystical word used by Gnostics to represent the supreme deity or the 365 heavens</note>; I set up these Basilidian gems <note>original: "Gemmas Basilidianas"; referring to the followers of Basilides, a 2nd-century Gnostic teacher in Alexandria</note> like trophies of bronze. They shall endure through the writings and monuments of the Holy Fathers for the perpetual memory of all time to come, to the praise of the Catholic Church, and to the shame of heresy.

->To Your Most Serene Highness<-

*Brussels, on the Calends of April <note>April 1st</note>, on the very day of Christ’s Resurrection <note>Easter Sunday</note>, in the year 1657.*

->Your most devoted Chaplain,<-
->*Jean Chiflet.*<-

<summary>In this formal dedication, Jean Chiflet presents his study of Gnostic "Abraxas" gems as a victory for the Catholic Church over ancient heresies. He dates the dedication to Easter Sunday, 1657, in Brussels.</summary>
<keywords>Abraxas, Basilidians, Gnosticism, Jean Chiflet, Brussels, Catholic Church, Counter-Reformation, amulets</keywords>

--- Page 19 ---
<meta>This page is the first illustrative plate following Jean Chiflet’s dedication. It provides visual evidence of the "Basilidian" gems he mentioned on the previous page, categorizing various magical amulets used by Gnostic sects.</meta>

# PLATE I

->(A full-page engraving of six mystical medallions)<-

1. **First Amulet (Two Sides)**
   **Front:** A solar deity <note>representing the sun or the Gnostic figure Abraxas</note> wearing a crown of radiant light and holding a whip is embraced or greeted by a lunar goddess with a crescent moon upon her head.
   **Back:** The seven Greek vowels <note>original: "ΑΕΗΙΟΥΩ" and "ΩΥΟΙΗΕΑ"; Gnostics believed these vowels represented the musical harmony of the seven planetary spheres</note> arranged in two vertical columns.

2. **Second Amulet**
   A winged figure—likely the child-god Harpocrates or Eros—riding a horse. He is flanked by the letters **IA** and **Ω** <term>Iao: a Greek rendering of the Hebrew Divine Name (Yahweh), frequently used in ancient magical charms</term>.

3. **Third Amulet**
   A standing nude male figure representing the Sun, wearing a crown of light and holding a whip <note>original: "flagellum"; a symbol of the sun-god driving his chariot</note> with a garment draped over his arm.

4. **Fourth Amulet**
   A standing nude solar figure with a radiant crown, holding a celestial orb <note>representing the universe or world dominion</note> in his right hand and a whip in his left.

5. **Fifth Amulet**
   A standing nude solar figure with a radiant crown, holding a whip and a long staff or scepter. He is flanked by two crescent moons, suggesting his power over both day and night.

<summary>This plate illustrates five distinct Gnostic amulets, featuring solar deities, the Greek name for God (Iao), and mystical vowel sequences used in ancient magic. These artifacts serve as the primary evidence for Jean Chiflet's study of the Basilidian heresy.</summary>
<keywords>Gnostic gems, amulets, Iao, solar deity, Abraxas, Greek vowels, Harpocrates, iconography, planetary spheres</keywords>

--- Page 20 ---
<meta>This page continues the visual catalog of Gnostic artifacts, moving from the solar deities of the first plate to the specific "anguipede" (serpent-legged) form of the deity Abraxas, which was a central figure in Basilidian and Gnostic magical practice.</meta>

# PLATE II

->(A full-page engraving of six mystical medallions featuring the serpent-legged deity Abraxas)<-

6. **Sixth Amulet**
   A figure of the deity Abraxas depicted as an <term>anguipede: a creature with the head of a rooster, the torso of a man, and legs that transition into two serpents</term>. He holds a wreath <note>a symbol of victory or divinity</note> in his right hand and a small globe or bag in his left.

7. **Seventh Amulet**
   The rooster-headed figure holds a whip and a shield inscribed with **I WA** <note>a variation of the Divine Name "Iao" or Yahweh</note>. He is surrounded by mystical Greek inscriptions: **IOX** and **OIOIX**, with **IAW** appearing in reverse on the right side. At the bottom are seven stars, likely representing the seven classical planets or the seven heavens of Gnostic cosmology.

8. **Eighth Amulet**
   The serpent-legged deity holds a flail or whip and a wreath inscribed with the letters **AI**. 

9. **Ninth Amulet**
   This variation of the deity holds a staff or whip and carries a large oval shield filled with several lines of <term>voces magicae: "magical voices" or secret words of power used in ancient spells</term>. The shield reads: **MAT, MVM, V X, LV M, COI**.

10. **Tenth Amulet**
    The figure holds a circular shield and a short sword or sacrificial knife. Above the head is the Greek letter **Ω** <note>Omega: the final letter of the Greek alphabet, often signifying the end or totality</note>, with the letters **I** and **A** to the left and right, completing the name **IAΩ** (Iao).

11. **Eleventh Amulet**
    The deity holds a knife and a shield inscribed with the letters **AI** and **Ω** (Omega). These characters are often used as shorthand for the divine name or to invoke specific celestial powers.

<summary>This plate illustrates six variations of the Abraxas figure, characterized by a rooster's head and serpent legs, carrying various weapons and shields of power. These amulets demonstrate the use of "voces magicae" and the Greek name for God (Iao) in Gnostic protective charms.</summary>

<keywords>Abraxas, anguipede, Gnostic gems, magical amulets, Iao, serpent-legged deity, Greek inscriptions, planetary stars</keywords>

--- Page 21 ---
<meta>This page continues the catalog of Gnostic amulets from Plate II, specifically focusing on the rooster-headed "anguipede" (serpent-legged) form of the deity Abraxas and the complex "voces magicae" (magical words) inscribed on the reverse of these gemstones.</meta>

# PLATE III

->(A full-page engraving showcasing three detailed sets of Gnostic amulets, featuring both the front and back of each stone)<-

12. **Twelfth Amulet**
    **Obverse:** The deity Abraxas is depicted as a <term>cock-headed god: a figure with the head of a rooster (symbolizing vigilance and the sun), a human torso in armor, and two serpents for legs</term>. He holds a shield in his right hand and a whip in his left, symbols of protection and divine authority. Five stars are scattered above him <note>representing the five planets known in antiquity besides the sun and moon</note>. To his side is the Greek letter **G** and the name **IAO** <note>original: ΙΑΩ — the Gnostic name for the supreme deity, derived from the Hebrew name of God</note>.
    **Reverse:** A large oval contains a grid of Greek characters: **G L / I Ch I L / K G Ch / E**. A smaller circular seal below repeats this same sequence. <note>These letter arrangements were often used as numerical codes or secret abbreviations to invoke specific angelic powers</note>.

13. **Thirteenth Amulet**
    **Obverse:** Another variation of the serpent-legged deity. Here, his shield is inscribed with the letters **I OA** <note>a variation of the name Iao</note>. At the base of his serpent tails are the Roman letters **F E O**. 
    **Reverse:** This stone is heavily inscribed with a ten-line magical formula and a vertical column of text on the right. The text includes strings such as **DKR**, **DIAX**, and **VAIVEITIE**. <note>These are "voces magicae" (magical voices) — phonetic strings of vowels and consonants that were believed to have direct power over the spiritual realm when spoken or worn as a charm</note>. The two sides of this amulet are joined by a decorative floral or cross-like ornament.

14. **Fourteenth Amulet**
    **Obverse:** The armored Abraxas figure carries a shield marked with the letters **A I Ω** <note>Alpha, Iota, and Omega — a combination of the first and last letters of the alphabet to signify the totality of God, paired with Iota for Iao</note>.
    **Reverse:** The oval contains five lines of text: **TALA ARAIO OARATHRO NTOKO NBAI** <note>original: ΤΑΛΑ ΑΡΑΙW ωΑΡΑΘΡΟ ΝΤΟΚΟ ΝΒΑΙ. This invocation likely includes a reference to "Barathron," the Greek term for the abyss or a deep pit, suggesting this was a binding spell intended to cast evil forces into the underworld</note>.

<summary>This plate illustrates three Gnostic amulets featuring the serpent-legged Abraxas, highlighting the transition from visual symbols of power to the use of complex, multi-line magical inscriptions known as voces magicae.</summary>

<keywords>Abraxas, Gnosticism, amulets, Iao, serpent-legged, cock-headed deity, voces magicae, Barathron, magical protection, Greek epigraphy</keywords>

->MICHAEL <note>original: ΜΙΧΑΗΛ</note><-

<note>This amulet consists of two joined medallions. The left side depicts the deity <term>Abraxas: a "cosmic" figure with the head of a rooster, a human body in armor, and legs made of serpents</term>. The right side, translated above, lists seven names. While Michael, Gabriel, Uriel, and Raphael are well-known archangels, names like Ananael and Prosoriel appear in Gnostic and apocryphal traditions, representing the seven planetary spheres.</note>

---

->16.<-

->IOUIOULIOUNV VO OV VWWNIOU <note>original: ΙΟΥΙΟΥΛΙΟΥΝV VO OV VWWΝΙΟΥ</note><-
->IAOLEE <note>original: ΙΑWΛΗΗ</note><-

<note>Item 16 features Abraxas on one side and a complex scene on the other involving a winged creature and a jackal-headed figure (likely Anubis, the Egyptian guide of souls). The text is a string of "voces magicae" (magical voices). Gnostics believed that certain combinations of vowels, like those seen here, carried the vibrational power of the heavens. "Iaolee" is a rhythmic expansion of the divine name Iao.</note>

---

->17.<-

->E ML <note>original: Η ΜΛ</note><-

<note>This oval gem depicts a long-necked bird, likely an Ibis, with a halo of light around its head. In this context, the Ibis—traditionally associated with the Egyptian god Thoth—represents divine wisdom and the sun. The letters "E ML" are likely abbreviations for a secret prayer or magical formula.</note>

---

->18.<-

<note>The final amulet on this plate shows the solar bird standing triumphantly upon a crocodile or lizard. This scene symbolizes the victory of light and spiritual intelligence over the "low" or "creeping" forces of the material world and chaos.</note>

<summary>This plate illustrates Gnostic amulets that blend Jewish angelic names (Michael, Gabriel) with Egyptian solar symbols (the Ibis) and the serpent-legged Abraxas. These items were worn for protection and to show the wearer's knowledge of the secret names that govern the universe.</summary>

# PLATE V

->(An engraved plate containing six oval medallions, depicting various protective gems or amulets, numbered 19 through 23)<-

---

->19.<-

->IAO ABRAXAS ADONAI <note>original: ΙΑW ΑΒΡΑCΑΖ ΑΔWΝΑΙ</note><-

<note>This item consists of two connected medallions, likely representing the front and back of a single gem. The left side depicts a bird, perhaps a raven or a crow, holding a leafy branch in its beak. The right side contains three powerful names of power. **Iao** is the Greek transliteration of the Hebrew name for God (Yahweh); **Abraxas** is the supreme Gnostic deity whose name's letters add up to 365, the days of the solar year; and **Adonai** is the Hebrew word for "Lord." Together, they form a "triple-threat" invocation for the wearer's protection.</note>

---

->20.<-

->MSF<-

<note>This oval gem shows a bird perched upon a branch. Above it are the letters MSF. In the world of ancient magic, such letters are often <term>charaktêres: cryptic symbols or abbreviations that were believed to hold secret meanings known only to the initiated or the spirits themselves</term>.</note>

---

->21.<-

->PVN<-

<note>Similar to item 20, this medallion depicts a bird on a branch with the letters PVN. These may be further shorthand for divine attributes or magical formulas intended to bring the favor of the heavens down to the earthly bird—a common symbol for the human soul.</note>

---

->22.<-

->(A bird with a star and a leafy branch)<-

<note>The bird here is accompanied by a star and a branch, likely symbolizing peace and celestial guidance. In the Mediterranean world, the raven was often associated with Apollo, the god of prophecy and light, which aligns with the Gnostic pursuit of "Gnosis" or divine knowledge.</note>

---

->23.<-

->(A bird standing on a branch)<-

<note>The final medallion in this set continues the theme of the bird as a messenger or symbolic guardian. These "raven gems" were frequently used as personal seals or protective charms to ward off bad luck and invite the watchful eyes of the divine.</note>

<summary>This plate illustrates a series of Gnostic amulets featuring bird imagery, specifically the raven, paired with the powerful "voces magicae" of Iao, Abraxas, and Adonai. These gems demonstrate the blending of Hebrew divine names with Greco-Egyptian animal symbolism to create protective charms.</summary>

# PLATE VI

->(An engraved plate containing six oval medallions, depicting various protective gems or amulets, numbered 24 through 27)<-

---

->24.<-

->IAO <note>original: ΙΑW</note><-

<note>This item shows both the front and back of a single amulet. On the left, a bearded, four-winged deity stands upon a base inscribed with the name **IAO** (a Greek rendering of the Hebrew name for God). The figure holds a scorpion—often a symbol of control over venomous forces—and a flail, an Egyptian sign of royal authority. On the right, the reverse of the gem provides a list of names: **Michael, Gabriel, and Kustiel** <note>original: MIXAHL, GABRIHL, KOUSTIHL</note>. In the ancient world, invoking the names of these Archangels was thought to create a shield of divine protection around the wearer.</note>

---

->25.<-

->(The Birth of the Sun)<-

<note>The left medallion shows a winged figure standing between two rocky peaks, a common depiction of the sun rising between the mountains of the horizon. The right medallion depicts **Harpocrates** <term>the Greek name for the Egyptian deity 'Horus-the-Child'</term>. He is shown seated upon a lotus flower, the symbol of creation, with his finger pressed to his lips. While later traditions interpreted this as a gesture of silence, to the ancients, it represented the quiet potential of the young sun at dawn.</note>

---

->26.<-

->IAO <note>original: WAI</note><-

<note>This gem features a winged figure wearing a <term>modius: a tall, basket-shaped headdress associated with divinity and the abundance of the earth</term>. He holds two long staffs or scepters. The letters surrounding him include a reversed spelling of **IAO** at his feet. The various Greek letters (H, N, A, M, O) scattered in the field likely refer to the "seven vowels" of the Greek alphabet, which Gnostics believed were the sounds through which the heavens were created.</note>

---

->27.<-

->(The Symbolic Protector)<-

<note>A complex and mysterious image featuring a bird-headed figure with arms outstretched. He is surrounded by a celestial star and various animals, including a serpent and a dog. This figure is often called a **Pantheos**, or "All-God," a composite deity that combines the powers of many different gods into one form to ensure the amulet is effective against any possible spiritual threat.</note>

<summary>This plate illustrates the "Pantheos" figures and solar amulets of the Gnostic tradition, featuring the names of Archangels and the deity Harpocrates. These gems demonstrate the blending of Jewish, Egyptian, and Greek religious elements into protective talismans.</summary>

# PLATE VII

->(An engraved plate containing three sets of circular and oval medallions, depicting various magical gems or amulets, numbered 28 through 30)<-

---

->28.<-

<note>This entry shows a solar amulet designed to harness the power of the constellation Leo. On the left (the obverse), a lion strides beneath a crescent moon and a six-pointed star, symbols of the celestial alignment. The lion, often associated with the sun’s strength, holds an object in its mouth, likely a ritual torch. On the right (the reverse), a protective inscription is encircled by mystical characters. The central text reads: **IAO** <note>original: ΙΑW—a Greek transcription of the Hebrew Name of God</note> and **ABRAXAS** <note>original: ABPACAZ—the supreme deity of the Gnostic system, whose name's letters numerically add up to 365, the days of the solar year</note>.</note>

---

->29.<-

<note>This complex amulet features a lion-headed figure, possibly the deity **Chnoubis**, standing upright as a guardian. On the reverse, we see a human figure whose body is inscribed with "vowel strings"—sequences like **I-E-U-O-Y-I-A** <note>original: IEYOYIA</note>. To the ancient Gnostic, these seven vowels represented the harmony of the seven planets and the secret, unutterable name of the Divine. Below his feet is the famous palindrome **ABLANATHANALBA** <note>original: ΑΒΛΑΝΑΘΑΝΑΛΒΑ</note>, a magical phrase derived from the Hebrew for "You are our Father." The vertical text on the right side invokes a long string of power-words, including the name **ABRAXAS**.</note>

---

->30.<-

<note>The final set on this plate depicts the most recognizable of Gnostic symbols. On the left, a lion walks surrounded by seven stars, representing the seven planetary spheres. On the right is the **Abraxas** figure in his most common form: having the head of a cock (symbolizing foresight and the dawn), the armored torso of a man, and legs made of serpents (representing the earth and vital energy). He carries a whip to drive away evil spirits. He stands before a smaller, kneeling figure, perhaps the person seeking protection. The inscriptions include **ATHAI** <note>original: ΑΘΑΙ</note> and a reversed version of the name of God, **IAO** <note>original: WAI</note>.</note>

<summary>This page illustrates gemstones featuring the "Lion-Gnostic" and "Abraxas" motifs, emphasizing the use of the Divine Name and planetary vowels as protective magic. These amulets were intended to grant the wearer authority over spiritual forces and the physical world through solar symbolism.</summary>
<keywords>Abraxas, Lion-headed deity, Chnoubis, IAO, Ablanathanalba, planetary vowels, Gnostic gems, solar amulets, magical protection</keywords>

# PLATE VIII

->(An engraved plate containing four numbered illustrations of circular and oval amulets, numbered 31 through 34)<-

---

->31.<-

<note>This circular gem depicts a lion in a mid-leap toward the right. The lion is a primary symbol of the sun's heat and the zodiacal sign of Leo, which was believed to govern physical health. Surrounding the beast are the letters **C**, **A**, **M**, and **A**, likely a shorthand for a magical name or an invocation for strength. Below the lion's belly is a small human face or ritual mask, which may represent the "spirit" of the sun or the person seeking the amulet's protection.</note>

---

->32.<-

<note>Similar to the previous figure, this circular gem shows a lion walking with purpose. Its front paw rests upon a small animal—perhaps a mouse or a lizard—symbolizing the sun's power to overcome small, creeping evils or diseases. Above the lion is the inscription **MAVI** <note>original: MAVI—a common magical word in Gnostic papyri, likely a variant of the divine name **IAO**</note>.</note>

---

->33.<-

<note>This entry shows both sides of an oval amulet joined by a decorative metal mount. On the left (the obverse), we see a lion-headed guardian standing upright. He carries a trident and a **situla** <term>a ritual bucket used for pouring libations</term>, blending the attributes of a solar deity with those of a priest. On the right (the reverse), a five-line inscription in Greek letters invokes secret names of power: **THOUTH** <note>original: ΘWΥΘ—the Egyptian god of wisdom and writing</note>, followed by the magical words **KOMBOUP**, **PHRIMNY**, **EMESIE**, and **METHI**. These "barbarous names" were believed to compel spirits through their sound rather than their literal meaning.</note>

---

->34.<-

<note>This final amulet depicts a specific form of the deity **Abraxas**. Unlike the more common rooster-headed version, this figure possesses the head of a lion, the torso of a man, and legs that turn into serpents. He holds a shield and a sword, ready for battle. The reverse side bears the Greek title **GIGANTORHEKTA** <note>original: ΓΙΓΑΝ / TOPH / KTA</note>, which translates to **"Slayer of the Giants."** This refers to the ancient myth of the gods defeating the chaotic Giants, signaling the wearer's belief that the Divine would similarly strike down the "giants" of illness, misfortune, or demonic interference.</note>

<summary>This plate catalogs four amulets featuring lion-headed imagery and the "Slayer of the Giants" epithet, highlighting the lion's role in Gnostic thought as a fierce protector against chaos and disease.</summary>
<keywords>Leo, lion-headed deity, Abraxas, Thoth, Gigantorekta, Slayer of Giants, situla, magical gems, talismans</keywords>

# PLATE IX

->(An engraved plate containing four numbered illustrations of circular and oval amulets, numbered 35 through 38)<-

---

->35.<-

<note>This entry shows two sides of a circular amulet. On the left, we see a youth, likely Harpocrates <note>original: Harpocrates—the Greek interpretation of the Egyptian "Horus-the-Child"</note>, seated upon a blooming **lotus** <term>a water lily representing the sun rising from the waters of creation</term>. He holds a flail or whip, a symbol of his authority over the heavens. To his left is a star and a crescent moon, and to his right, a lizard—an animal believed to seek out the sun to restore its sight. On the right side, the gem is filled with a dense magical inscription in Greek letters. The central part reads: **SOUMARTA**, **ABLANATHANALBA** <note>original: ΑΒΛΑΝΑΘΑΝΑΛΒΑ—a famous palindrome meaning 'Thou art our Father'</note>, and **KRAMACHAMARI** <note>original: ΚΡΑΜΑΧΑΜΑΡΙ—a magical name often used to invoke the solar light</note>. The outer rings are filled with symbolic characters and vowels used in Gnostic chanting.</note>

---

->36.<-

<note>This oval gem depicts a draped figure holding a long staff. The figure is surrounded by several **characteres** <term>abstract, star-tipped symbols used in ancient magic to represent planetary spirits or secret names of God</term>. Unlike the more common solar figures, this represents a more celestial or priestly aspect of the divine, perhaps an intermediary between the heavens and the wearer of the amulet.</note>

---

->37.<-

<note>Here is another variation of the youthful sun god seated on a lotus flower. He holds a finger to his lips, a gesture that Greeks interpreted as a command for "silence" regarding sacred mysteries, though in Egyptian art it simply designated him as a child. He is flanked by two additional lotus buds, emphasizing the theme of recurring growth and the daily rebirth of the sun.</note>

---

->38.<-

<note>This final pair shows a youth on the left (the obverse) wearing a crown and carrying a victory wreath, emphasizing his role as the conqueror of darkness. On the right (the reverse), the Greek inscription reads **GE MES EILAMPS** <note>original: ΓΕ ΜΕΣ ΕΙΛΑΜΨ—likely a corruption of the Hebrew *Shemesh Eilam*, meaning 'Eternal Sun'</note>. This confirms the solar nature of these charms, which were intended to bring the wearer the "light" and "victory" of the sun in their personal affairs.</note>

<summary>This plate documents Gnostic amulets featuring Harpocrates, the infant sun god, seated on a lotus. These artifacts highlight the use of the "eternal sun" motif and palindromic magical formulas like Ablanathanalba for spiritual protection and rebirth.</summary>
<keywords>Harpocrates, lotus, Gnostic gems, Ablanathanalba, solar deity, magical inscriptions, Shemesh Eilam, Horus-the-child</keywords>

# PLATE X

->(An engraved copperplate featuring three numbered sets of Gnostic amulets, showing both obverse and reverse faces, numbered 39 through 41.)<-

***

->39.<-

<note>This entry illustrates the front and back of an oval amulet. On the left (the obverse), we see the figure of **Harpocrates** <note>original: Harpocrates—the Greek name for the Egyptian 'Heru-pa-khered' or Horus-the-Child</note> seated upon a blooming **lotus** flower. He holds a flail, a symbol of his royal power to drive the heavens. Surrounding him are the letters **C A B A**, likely a shortened form of **Sabaoth** <term>a Hebrew title for God meaning 'of Hosts'</term>, and a small **E**. On the right (the reverse), the name **IAO** <note>original: ΙΑΩ—the Gnostic Greek transcription of the four-letter Hebrew name of God, Yahweh</note> is inscribed within a protective border. The gem serves as a plea for the creator's protection, invoking the morning sun's renewal.</note>

***

->40.<-

<note>This elaborate gem combines several potent symbols. On the front, Harpocrates is again seated on his lotus, but the flower itself is perched upon the back of a **lion** walking toward the right. Above the lion’s head is a star, representing the sun's position in the constellation of Leo, the height of its strength. The name **ABRACAZ** <note>original: ΑΒΡΑΣΑΞ—Abraxas, a mystical word whose letters add up to 365 in Greek numerology, representing the cycle of the year</note> and the word **SISI** <note>original: ΣΙΣΙ</note> encircle the image. Below the lion is the word **WIP**.

The reverse side contains a longer Greek inscription: **ARCHE O IAO MERM ENN O** <note>original: ΑΡΧΕ Ο ΙΑΩ ΜΕΡΜ ΕΝΝ Ω—'The Beginning is Iao' or 'Rule, O Iao'</note>. This amulet was likely intended to harness the supreme authority of the solar ruler to command spirits or ensure the wearer’s dominance over their circumstances.</note>

***

->41.<-

<note>The final set on this plate shows Harpocrates in his most characteristic pose, seated on the lotus with one finger pressed to his lips. This gesture, which the Greeks interpreted as a command for **silence** and the keeping of sacred mysteries, actually originated from the Egyptian artistic convention of representing a child by showing them sucking their thumb. On the reverse, the name of God is reversed as **WAI** <note>original: ΩΑΙ—a common magical practice of reversing names to change their power</note>, accompanied by several **characteres** <term>mystical, star-tipped symbols that functioned as a secret alphabet of the spirits</term>.</note>

<summary>This plate illustrates three Gnostic gems featuring Harpocrates (the child Horus) on a lotus, emphasizing his role as the solar creator and his association with the names Iao and Abraxas.</summary>
<keywords>Harpocrates, Horus, Lotus, Iao, Abraxas, Lion, Leo, Sabaoth, silence, magical symbols, solar deity, Gnosticism</keywords>

# PLATE XI

->(An engraved copperplate featuring several oval amulets and medallions, numbered 42 through 45, illustrating various Gnostic and Egyptianizing protective symbols.)<-

***

->42.<-

->Θ X<-
<note>original: Θ Χ — The Greek letters Theta and Chi.</note>

<note>This oval gem depicts a crowned male figure standing in a formal pose. In his right hand, he holds a **chalice** <note>possibly a ritual vessel or a symbol of the soul's reception of divine grace</note>. To his left is the letter **Theta** (Θ) and to his right is the letter **Chi** (Χ). In the context of early mystical gems, these letters often served as shorthand for divine titles or protective "characters" meant to invoke heavenly powers.</note>

***

->43.<-

<note>This entry displays two connected medallions. The upper oval features a complex symbolic object, perhaps a sacred vessel or an altar, from which rays of light emerge. It is flanked at the base by two figures with animal heads, reminiscent of the Egyptian jackal-headed Anubis or perhaps the donkey-headed figures sometimes associated with "Typhonian" or Gnostic depictions of the demiurge. 

Connected below is a smaller oval containing the sacred name:</note>

->I A Ω<-
<note>original: ΙΑΩ — Iao, the Greek phonetic rendering of the Hebrew Tetragrammaton (the four-letter name of God, Yahweh). In Gnostic thought, Iao was often considered the ruler of one of the celestial spheres.</note>

***

->44.<-

<note>This set of medallions combines Egyptian funerary motifs with magical names. The upper oval contains a mummiform figure—likely **Osiris**—alongside a **scarab** <term>a beetle representing rebirth and the morning sun</term>, an ibis or hawk representing **Thoth** or **Horus**, and a serpent. 

The lower oval, connected by a decorative hinge, bears a three-line invocation:</note>

->I A Ω<-
->C A B<-
->P A Ω<-

<note>original: ΙΑΩ ΣΑΒ ΡΑΩ — Iao Sab[aoth] Rao. This is a common magical formula. **Sabaoth** is a Hebrew title meaning "of Hosts." The word **Rao** is often found in these gems as a rhythmic or mystical completion of the name, emphasizing the authority of the deity over the heavens.</note>

***

->45.<-

<note>This larger circular medallion is divided into three registers, or horizontal sections, representing different realms of influence. 

In the **top register**, a central figure sits enthroned, flanked by two horsemen who appear to be the **Dioscuri** <note>the twins Castor and Pollux, who were viewed as protectors of travelers</note> or perhaps solar attendants. 

The **middle register** shows figures traveling in a **solar barque** or boat, a classic Egyptian image of the sun's journey through the sky or the underworld. 

The **bottom register** is filled with various "characteres" <term>magical signs or alphabets not belonging to any known language</term>, a bird, and a standing figure. Such medallions were often worn as phylacteries <note>protective charms</note> to ensure safe passage and divine favor in both life and the afterlife.</note>

<summary>This plate illustrates a variety of Gnostic amulets that blend Egyptian religious symbols (like Osiris and the solar barque) with Greek and Hebrew divine names (Iao Sabaoth). These objects demonstrate how ancient practitioners combined different traditions to create powerful protective magic.</summary>

<keywords>Iao, Sabaoth, Gnosticism, amulets, Osiris, solar barque, magical gems, Greek inscriptions, Egyptian iconography</keywords>

# PLATE XII

->(An engraved plate featuring four distinct sets of Gnostic amulets and magical seals, numbered 46 through 49.)<-

***

->46.<-

<note>This entry displays the two faces of a circular medal or amulet. 

On the left face, two figures stand in profile. The figure on the left is human-headed and carries a long staff topped with a bird—likely a **phoenix** or a **falcon**, symbols of solar renewal. Facing him is the jackal-headed **Anubis**, the Egyptian guide of souls. Between them is a vertical inscription: 

**Iao Phren Phero** 
<note>original: ΙΑΩ ΦCPEN ΦEPW — This is a powerful magical formula. **Iao** is the Greek transliteration of the Hebrew name for God (Yahweh). **Phren** likely refers to the Egyptian sun-god Ra (Phre) or the Greek word for "mind/soul." **Phero** is Greek for "I carry" or "I bring." Together, the phrase may be an invocation to the Sun-God who "brings the light."</note>

The right face of the medal contains three lines of **magical characters** <term>also called "charakteres," these are non-linguistic symbols or "alphabetical ghosts" believed to represent divine forces or planetary spirits</term>.</note>

***

->47.<-

<note>This illustration shows two hexagonal, faceted gems joined by a decorative ribbon tied in a bow. 

The gem on the left features a seated figure with hands raised to its head in a gesture of mourning or deep contemplation. Below the figure are the Greek letters **IXΞΓ** <note>original: IXΞΓ — Likely a numerical code or a cryptic abbreviation common in Gnostic talismans; in Greek gematria, letters often functioned as numbers to hide sacred names.</note>. The gem on the right is blank, suggesting a paired set where only one stone was engraved.</note>

***

->48.<-

<note>A circular seal centered on the figure of **Anubis**. Here, the jackal-headed god is not merely a guide but is seated upon a throne, assuming the status of a celestial judge or ruler. The chair rests upon a heavy base. In the space surrounding him are three symbolic elements: 

1. A **caduceus** <note>the winged staff of Hermes, signifying the blending of Egyptian Anubis with the Greek Hermes into the figure of "Hermanubis"</note>.
2. A **prostrate human figure** lying face down in a gesture of worship or submission.
3. A **crab**, which likely represents the zodiac sign of **Cancer**, indicating a specific astrological timing or a connection to the moon and the gateway of souls.</note>

***

->49.<-

<note>This oval seal depicts a complex mythological scene. At the center stands a **three-headed monster** or composite deity. It stands atop a platform supported by a tree-like structure with visible roots, which may represent the **Axis Mundi** or the Tree of Life. 

Flanking this central pillar are two smaller human figures in a posture of adoration. To the left of the trunk is the letter **O** <note>original: Ω — the Greek letter Omega</note> and to the right are the letters **AI**. These letters are likely fragments of the name **Adonai** or part of a vowel-based musical invocation used to ascend the celestial spheres.</note>

***

<summary>This plate illustrates the "Hermanubis" tradition and the use of the "Iao Phren" formula, demonstrating how Gnostic practitioners blended Egyptian funerary gods with Greek magical theory.</summary>

<keywords>Anubis, Iao Phren Phero, magical characters, Hermanubis, caduceus, Gnostic gems, Cancer zodiac, three-headed deity, magical amulets</keywords>

# PLATE XIII

->**Table XIII**<-

***

->50.<-

<note>This vignette depicts a standing dog-headed figure, a classic representation of **Anubis**, the Egyptian god of the underworld and the "Opener of the Ways." He wears a short tunic and holds a staff topped with a ram’s head <note>The ram's head likely refers to the god Khnum or the solar aspect of Osiris, symbolizing creation and rebirth.</note>. In his other hand, he grasps a sword or dagger, marking him as a protective guardian against malevolent spirits.</note>

***

->51.<-

<note>A dog-headed figure, possibly representing **Thoth** in his canine aspect rather than his more common ibis form. He is crowned with a crescent moon, identifying him as a lunar deity and the keeper of time. He holds a flowering branch or scepter and a **situla** <term>a ceremonial bucket used for carrying sacred water or milk in temple rituals</term>. This image emphasizes the deity’s role in ritual purification.</note>

***

->52.<-

<note>A profile view of a dog-headed figure dressed in a long, priestly robe with a cross-hatched bodice. He carries a small ritual vessel. This depiction illustrates the "humanization" of Egyptian gods during the later periods, where they were often portrayed as officiating priests in their own mysteries.</note>

***

->53.<-

<note>A dog-headed deity seated upon a cubic throne. He wears a complex crown, likely a variation of the **Hemhem** crown <note>original: A multi-tiered crown often worn by solar or child-deities, representing divine power and triumph.</note>. Holding a long staff or flail, the deity is shown here in a judicial or royal capacity, reflecting the belief in Anubis as the judge of souls who weighs hearts in the Hall of Truth.</note>

***

->54.<-

<note>This figure represents **Hermanubis**, a powerful syncretic deity who blended the Greek god **Hermes** with the Egyptian **Anubis**. As the messenger between the world of the living and the dead, he carries the **caduceus** <term>the winged staff entwined with two snakes, the traditional symbol of Hermes</term> and a palm branch, symbolizing victory over death. Below him, faint traces of magical lettering suggest a hidden invocation meant to secure safe passage for the soul.</note>

***

->55.<-

<note>A complex Gnostic scene featuring a two-headed figure—one head human and the other animal—holding a palm branch and a flaming torch. He is surrounded by **seven stars**, which represent the seven classical planets or the seven archons who rule the celestial spheres in Gnostic cosmology. To the right, a human head emerges from the ground or water beneath a crescent, possibly representing the rising of the soul or the process of spiritual awakening under the influence of the heavens.</note>

***

<summary>This plate documents the diverse iconography of dog-headed deities in Gnostic gems, specifically the evolution of Anubis into the syncretic Hermanubis, acting as a guide and protector through the celestial spheres.</summary>

# PLATE XIV

->**Table XIV**<-

***

->56.<-

<note>This oval medallion depicts the goddess **Hecate**, often called *Triformis* or "Three-formed." She is shown here with three heads and six arms, holding a pair of torches and daggers. In the Gnostic tradition, Hecate was reimagined as a powerful celestial entity who guarded the boundaries between the worlds and could assist the soul in its ascent through the planetary spheres.</note>

***

->58.<-

<note>A seated **cynocephalus** <term>a baboon-headed figure, representing the Egyptian god Thoth as a recorder of time and wisdom</term>. He wears a lunar crescent upon his head, signifying his control over the moon’s phases. He is flanked by the letters **I** and **A**, with the Greek letter **Ω** (Omega) below. Together, these likely abbreviate "Iao," the secret name of the Gnostic Demiurge or the Supreme God, signaling that this image served as a potent protective seal.</note>

***

->[Middle Left - Unnumbered]<-

<note>A youth, likely **Harpocrates** (the Greek name for the infant Horus), sits upon a lotus flower, which in Egyptian mythology represents the birth of the sun from the primeval waters. He holds a flail—a symbol of sovereignty—and is surrounded by the inscription **IAW C ABANW** <note>original: "ΙΑΩ C ΑΒΑΝΩ" – likely a Greek transliteration of the Hebrew *Iao Sabaoth*, meaning "Lord of Hosts."</note>. The surrounding stars and celestial symbols identify him as a ruler of the cosmos.</note>

***

->59.<-

<note>A complex scene depicting a **solar bark** <term>a divine boat used by the sun god to travel across the sky</term>. At the center, Harpocrates sits upon a lotus flower. He is attended by a baboon (Thoth) and a hawk (Horus), representing wisdom and solar power. Above the bark are the sun, the crescent moon, and a star, indicating that this amulet was intended to harmonize the wearer with the entire celestial order.</note>

***

->57.<-

<note>A multi-armed deity, possibly another variation of Hecate or a "pantheistic" Gnostic spirit, stands holding torches and serpents. Below the figure is a dense Greek inscription: **ΑΜΟΙΡΙΜΟΙΡΡΙΟΜΙΡΙΟΜ / ΜΗΖΙΙΖΖΟΙΖ / UVΞΕVΜΟΖ**. <note>original: "Amoirimoirriomirriom..." – these are *voces magicae*, or magical names, which often use palindromes and rhythmic repetitions to create a "language of the gods" that transcends human speech.</note></note>

***

->60.<-

<note>A standing figure, possibly a magician or a protective spirit, holds two serpents horizontally. A bird—perhaps a hawk or phoenix—is perched upon their head, symbolizing divine inspiration or the soul. Below the figure is a tablet containing a string of Greek vowels: **ΙΑΙΗΟVWVΟΙΗ** <note>The seven Greek vowels were frequently used in Gnostic magic to represent the seven planetary spheres and their governing angels.</note>. The symbols below, including the cross and various sigils, are "charaktêres" or occult signs believed to carry specific supernatural charges.</note>

<summary>This plate illustrates the syncretism of Egyptian, Greek, and Jewish elements in Gnostic amulets, featuring Hecate, Harpocrates on the lotus, and complex magical inscriptions used for protection and spiritual ascent.</summary>

# PLATE XV

->**Table XV**<-

***

->61.<-

<note>This entry shows two faces of a single gem depicting the deity **Abraxas**, the supreme ruler of the 365 heavens in Gnostic cosmology. On the left face, he is shown as a *pantheos* <term>a composite deity combining many divine attributes</term>: he has the head of a rooster (symbolizing the sun and vigilance), a human torso clad in armor, and legs made of serpents that curve upward (representing the earthly and the divine). He holds a whip, signifying his command over the celestial horses, and a shield for defense. Around him are the Greek letters **PHEONTSZ** <note>original: ΦΕΟΝϹΖ</note>, likely a mystical vowel formula or a code for the planetary rulers. The right face depicts a landscape or sanctuary with the inscription **SAWA** <note>original: "ΣΑΩΑ" – likely a variant of the Hebrew *Sabaoth*, meaning "of Hosts," a title of God used to invoke divine power.</note>.</note>

***

->62.<-

<note>These two faces represent a synthesis of Egyptian and Greco-Roman protective magic. The left face shows a **chalice** or altar flanked by two protective serpents and celestial bows, suggesting a ritual vessel for the soul. The right face contains a dense symbolic scene: two mounted riders—potentially the **Dioscuri** <term>the twin brothers Castor and Pollux, protectors of travelers</term>—approach a central figure. Above them are the sun, moon, and stars, while below a jackal or dog (perhaps the god **Anubis**) sits near a ritual cup. Such gems were often worn to ensure safety during travel and the favor of the stars.</note>

***

->63.<-

<note>This oval gem depicts **Chnoubis**, a powerful Gnostic spirit. He is represented as a serpent with the head of a lion, crowned with seven solar rays. In the ancient world, Chnoubis was primarily a deity of health and protection; his image was frequently engraved on green jasper and worn to cure stomach ailments or to protect the wearer from poison. His form combines the solar majesty of the lion with the regenerative power of the snake.</note>

***

->64.<-

<note>A variation of the **Chnoubis** amulet. While similar to the previous figure, this serpent is positioned beneath a **lunar crescent** with a six-pointed star at the base of its coils. The addition of the moon suggests a desire to balance solar and lunar energies, or perhaps a focus on the "sublunary" world—the realm of physical life and change where the wearer sought protection.</note>

<summary>Plate XV illustrates the most recognizable Gnostic protective figures, including the rooster-headed Abraxas and the lion-headed serpent Chnoubis. These gems served as physical talismans meant to harness cosmic authority for the wearer's health and safety.</summary>

<keywords>Abraxas, Chnoubis, Gnostic gems, amulets, Sabaoth, solar rays, Anubis, Dioscuri, magical vowels, protection</keywords>

Within the circle of the serpent are three **sigils** <term>mystical symbols or "characters" believed to hold the secret names of angels or demons</term>. The right face contains a long string of Greek vowels.</note>

->**IAEIEIOUŌEIE**<-
<note>original: "ΙΑΗΙΕΗΙΟΥΩΗΙΗ" — This is a classic example of Gnostic "vowel magic." The seven vowels of the Greek alphabet were thought to correspond to the seven planets and their presiding spirits; chanting or inscribing them in specific patterns was believed to invoke celestial power.</note>

***

->66.<-

<note>A single circular amulet bounded by an Ouroboros. The text inside consists of *voces magicae* <term>magical words or "barbarous names" that carry power through their sound rather than their literal meaning</term>.</note>

<note>original: "ΧΛΧΙ"</note>
->**NLLLB**<-
<note>original: "ΝΛΛΛΒ" (written in inverted characters) — Inverting or mirroring text was a common technique in ancient magic to "bind" a spirit or to signal that the text was intended for the supernatural world rather than human eyes.</note>

<note>A smaller circular amulet with an Ouroboros. The interior contains fragmented characters, likely abbreviations for divine names.</note>

<note>original: "Ι ΜΧ"</note>
->**ELL**<-
<note>original: "ΗΛΛ" — This may be a derivative of "El," the Hebrew word for God, frequently incorporated into Gnostic charms.</note>

<note>original: "ΙΕΟΛΜΗΙ"</note>
->**IVHIEH**<-
<note>original: "IVHIEH" — This likely represents a variation of the Tetragrammaton, the four-letter unpronounceable name of God in the Hebrew tradition.</note>

<note>original: "IH"</note>

<note>original: "V □ I X X C RI" — These are part of a series of **charaktêres** <term>non-linguistic magical symbols believed to hold the secret power of planetary spirits or angels</term>.</note>

# PLATE XIX

->**Table XIX**<-

***

->**76.**<-

<note>This elaborate amulet features the lion-headed serpent Chnoubis, but adds a series of powerful titles and solar declarations that designate the figure as a supreme cosmic authority.</note>

**Obverse:**
<note>A lion-headed serpent (Chnoubis) with a solar halo. Surrounding the figure are several Greek invocations.</note>

**Beropheibarraphaiantes**
<note>original: "ΒΕΡΟΦΕΙΒΑΡΒΑΦΑΙΑΝΤΗΣ" — A magical name of power, likely derived from Hebrew or Aramaic roots meaning "Lord of the Heavens" or "Spirit of the High Places."</note>

**Slayer of Giants**
<note>original: "ΓΙΓΑΝΤΟΠΤΟΡΗΚΤΑ" (Gigantoptorekta) — A title referring to the deity’s role in overcoming chaotic, earthly forces (the Giants), likening the solar god to Zeus or Apollo in their battle against primordial chaos.</note>

**Cholchnoubis**
<note>original: "ΧΟΛΧΝΟΥΒΙC" — A variant form of **Chnoubis** <term>the solar-serpent deity of health and protection</term>.</note>

**I am the Eternal Sun**
<note>original: "ΑΝΟΧ CEMECEILAM" — "Anoch" is a Coptic/Hebrew word for "I am," and "Semes Eilam" (Shemesh Olam) means "Eternal Sun." Together, they form a statement of the deity’s self-revelation.</note>

**Iao**
<note>original: "IAW" — The Greek transcription of the Hebrew tetragrammaton (YHWH), used here as a supreme name of power to seal the magic.</note>

**Reverse:**
<note>A grid containing nine **charaktêres** <term>magical, non-linguistic signs believed to be the secret scripts of angels or planetary spirits</term>.</note>

***

->**77.**<-

<note>This talisman moves away from the serpent form to a **mummiform** <term>shaped like a mummy or a wrapped figure</term> deity, often representing Osiris or a Gnostic soul perfected and ready for ascent.</note>

**Obverse:**
<note>A mummiform figure standing on a pedestal, arms crossed in a gesture of silence or authority. The figure is crowned and surrounded by stars and magical symbols.</note>

**Reverse:**
<note>A complex array of symbols: a globe with a grid representing the cosmos, a compass and square suggesting divine architecture, and various geometric patterns used to bind or direct spiritual energy.</note>

***

->**78.**<-

<note>This final pair reinforces the solar and celestial themes, depicting the mummiform figure as a ruler of the heavens, supported by angelic beings.</note>

**Obverse:**
<note>The mummiform figure stands above two winged genii or angels who are seated upon a celestial globe, signifying the deity’s rule over the moving spheres of the stars.</note>

**I Trust** / **Michael**
<note>original: "INVTIQ, MIVHIXI" — These appear to be phonetic variations or corruptions of Greek and Hebrew invocations, possibly referencing the Archangel Michael or the practitioner's faith.</note>

**Reverse:**
<note>The mummiform figure is repeated, flanked by two large, prominent magical characters and encircled by a ring of stars, representing the fixed firmament.</note>

***

These gems combine solar "names of power" like Semes Eilam with complex geometric symbols and angelic imagery to provide cosmic protection.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the catalog of mystical and celestial engravings. While Plate XIX focused on the specific solar-serpent deity Chnoubis and his powerful vocalizations, Plate XX shifts toward more complex astronomical and astrological diagrams. These figures represent the macrocosm—the larger celestial order—which the individual soul must navigate according to Gnostic and late-antique thought.</meta>

# PLATE XX

->**Table XX**<-

***

->**79.**<-

<note>This oval medallion focuses on the central power of the Sun, depicted here with a human face to represent the guiding intelligence of the cosmos.</note>

**Sun**
<note>original: "Sol"</note>
The central figure is a solar face with radiating beams of light. Surrounding this central authority are several celestial companions: a large star dominates the upper position, while the symbols for **Venus** <term>the planet associated with love and harmony</term> and **Mars** <term>the planet of war and vitality</term> appear on the sides. Two smaller stars and a crescent **Moon** <note>original: "Luna"</note> at the bottom complete this map of the visible heavens.

***

->**80.**<-

<note>A companion piece to the previous medallion, this diagram shifts the focus from the solar day to the lunar night.</note>

**Moon**
<note>original: "Luna"</note>
A large crescent moon sits at the center of this oval. It is surrounded by an eight-pointed star at the top and four smaller stars around the perimeter, representing the night sky as an orderly, protective dome.

***

->**81.**<-

<note>This circular diagram demonstrates the intersection of mythology and astrology, placing the god of war at the center of the turning year.</note>

**Mars and the Zodiac**
In the center stands a Roman soldier representing **Mars** <term>the god of war and agriculture</term>, holding a spear and a shield. The outer ring is divided into twelve segments containing the signs of the **Zodiac** <term>the "circle of animals" or path of the sun through the heavens</term>.

***

->**82.**<-

<note>A more naturalistic or "vegetal" astrological diagram, emphasizing the Moon's influence over the earth and the seasons.</note>

**Lunar Cycle and the Stars**
The central motif features a crescent moon, a single star, and floral sprigs. The outer ring is divided into twelve segments. Among the figures and animals depicted are **Scorpio** <note>the Scorpion</note> and **Libra** <note>the Scales</note>, highlighting the transition from autumn into the darker half of the year.

***

->**83.**<-

<note>This complex double-circular diagram represents a "hinge" or connection between different celestial systems—one focusing on the calendar year and the other on the planetary deities.</note>

**The Cosmic Hinge**
The left circle contains a central sun or star and is divided into multiple concentric rings. The outer ring displays the full progression of the **Zodiac**:
*   **Aries** <note>the Ram</note>
*   **Taurus** <note>the Bull</note>
*   **Gemini** <note>the Twins</note>
*   **Cancer** <note>the Crab</note>
*   **Leo** <note>the Lion</note>
*   **Virgo** <note>the Virgin</note>
*   **Libra** <note>the Scales</note>
*   **Scorpio** <note>the Scorpion</note>
*   **Sagittarius** <note>the Archer</note>
*   **Capricorn** <note>the Goat-Horned</note>
*   **Aquarius** <note>the Water-Bearer</note>
*   **Pisces** <note>the Fish</note>
The inner rings contain various letters and symbols.

The right circle features a central smooth **cabochon** <term>a gemstone shaped and polished into a rounded dome</term> or sphere. The surrounding ring contains various mythological figures representing planetary deities and constellations, including the symbols for **Venus**, the **Sun**, and **Mars**.

<summary>This plate illustrates a collection of astrological and astronomical diagrams that map the heavens using the Sun, Moon, and the twelve signs of the Zodiac. These artifacts demonstrate how late-antique practitioners visualized the celestial forces that governed both the passage of time and the destinies of men.</summary>

<keywords>Zodiac, Mars, Venus, Sun, Moon, Astrology, Planetary Deities, Astronomy, Gnostic Gems, Celestial Mapping</keywords>

--- Page 39 ---
<meta>This page continues the catalog of mystical and celestial engravings. While Plate XX focused on the broader celestial order—the Sun, Moon, and the Zodiac surrounding Mars—Plate XXI moves into specific planetary talismans associated with the names of archangels and the personification of classical virtues. These figures illustrate the syncretism of Greek mythology, Roman astrology, and Judeo-Christian angelic lore common in late antiquity and the Renaissance.</meta>

# PLATE XXI

->**Plate XXI**<-

***

->**84.**<-

<note>This circular talisman unites the Roman god of time with an angelic guardian, creating a protective charm influenced by the planet Saturn.</note>

**Saturn and the Angel Satoviel**
A bearded figure, representing **Saturn** <term>the Roman god of time, agriculture, and the furthest of the classical planets</term>, sits in a position of authority. He carries a scythe, a traditional symbol of the harvest and the relentless passage of time, and a staff. Above him is the sign for **Sagittarius** <term>the Archer</term>, and to his right is the symbol for **Jupiter** <term>the king of the planets</term> accompanied by an eagle, Jupiter’s sacred bird. On the left, the name **Satoviel** <note>original: "SATOVIEL"</note> is written vertically; this is a magical or angelic name likely derived from "Saturn" to invoke the planet's spiritual essence.

***

->**85.**<-

<note>This image depicts the messenger of the gods under the protection of the highest archangel, often invoked for communication and wisdom.</note>

**Mercury and the Archangel Michael**
The winged figure of **Mercury** <term>the messenger god, known as Hermes in Greek</term> sits holding the **caduceus** <term>a staff entwined with two snakes, representing balance and trade</term>. Before him stands a rooster, an animal sacred to Mercury as the herald of the new day. Surrounding him are various alchemical and astrological symbols, including the crescent moon and the sign for Mercury. The name **Michael** <note>original: "MICHAEL", written in reverse as "LEAHCIM"</note> is inscribed vertically. In this tradition, Michael is often called upon to govern the celestial movements or protect the practitioner from wandering spirits.

***

->**86.**<-

<note>Moving from planetary gods to classical ideals, this talisman invokes the social graces and harmony.</note>

**The Three Graces**
Three female figures stand together, representing the **Three Graces** <term>known as the Charites in Greek mythology: Aglaia (Splendor), Euphrosyne (Mirth), and Thalia (Good Cheer)</term>. They symbolize everything that is beautiful and harmonious in human life. Encircling them is the cryptic inscription **SYCNOCVSAL** <note>possibly a magical "Voces Magicae" or a corrupted Greek phrase intended to seal the talisman's power</note>. Below their feet are the letters **W V**, likely a shorthand for a dedicatory formula or the initials of a specific practitioner.

***

->**87.**<-

<note>This talisman focuses on the feminine power of the Moon, connecting the lunar cycle to the announcement of divine mysteries.</note>

**The Moon and the Archangel Gabriel**
A seated female figure represents **Luna** <note>original: "Luna", the Roman personification of the Moon</note> or **Diana** <term>the goddess of the hunt and the moon</term>. She wears a crown shaped like a crescent moon and holds a scepter of rule. Above her is the sign of **Cancer** <term>the Crab</term>, which in astrology is the "house" or sign ruled by the Moon. The name **Gabriel** <note>original: "GABRIEL"</note> is written on the left; Gabriel is traditionally the archangel of the Moon, the messenger who brings hidden knowledge to light.

***

->**88.**<-

<note>These dual medallions represent the "watery" signs of the zodiac, often used in talismans meant to influence emotions, healing, or the secrets of the deep.</note>

**The Watery Signs and the Luminaries**
This pair of oval medallions displays a cluster of astrological influences. On the left, a large **Scorpio** <term>the Scorpion</term> is shown beneath a complex sigil <term>a magical symbol or seal</term>. On the right, the heavens are crowded with the other water signs: the two fish of **Pisces** and the crayfish or lobster representing **Cancer**. Hovering above them are the two "luminaries": the **Sun** <note>original: "Sol"</note> and the **Moon** <note>original: "Luna"</note>, here depicted with a human face to show its sentient, governing power over the tides and the human spirit.

<summary>This page catalogs five magical talismans that blend Roman planetary gods (Saturn, Mercury, Luna) with their corresponding archangels (Satoviel, Michael, Gabriel) and zodiacal signs. These engravings illustrate how ancient practitioners attempted to harness celestial power through visual symbols and sacred names.</summary>

<keywords>Saturn, Mercury, Three Graces, Luna, Archangels, Michael, Gabriel, Satoviel, Zodiac, Scorpio, Cancer, Pisces, Talismans, Astrological Sigils</keywords>
