...to congratulate you on your profit, and finally to break out into those cheers and cries,

<margin>Juvenal, Satire 8.</margin>
> — which the people shout, *Osiris*

*having been found*, <note>original: "Osiri Inuento"</note> through the happy wisdom of the Dog-headed One <note>The Cynocephalus, an Egyptian deity associated with Anubis or Thoth, often linked to the discovery of Osiris.</note>. Plautus says: *Boys* <margin>Act 5, Asinaria, Scene 2.</margin> APPLAUD, and *give me* FOR THE THROW *a drink of honeyed wine from the jug*. Among the Romans, sometimes, *when the dice were thrown, whenever someone* <margin>Suetonius in his Life of Augustus.</margin> *had cast a* DOG <note>The "Dog" (Canis) was the lowest, most losing throw in ancient dice games.</note> *or a six, they would contribute a single denarius for each die; all of which was taken by the one who* *had thrown* Venus <note>The "Venus" throw was the highest possible result, where all four dice showed different numbers.</note>. Hence the ruinous DOGS in Ovid, and likewise in Proper- <margin>Book 2, On the Art of Love.</margin> tius, for whom the die of Venus is also favorable:

<margin>Book 4, Elegy 9.</margin>
> *While I too was seeking a* FAVORABLE Venus *through the* DICE,
> *The* RUINOUS DOGS *always leaped up at me.*

This is contrary to what happened among the Egyptians, for whom the face of the Dog-headed One was most fortunate. And indeed, regarding this practice of dice within sacred rites themselves, Pausanias writes: *Where one descends to the sea, there is a river* <margin>Book 7, on Achaia.</margin> *named Buraicus. In a nearby cave there is a statue of Hercules, not particularly large, also surnamed Buraicus: the* ORACLE'S LOTS <note>original: "SORTES"</note> *are taken from a tablet by means of* DICE <note>original: "TALOS" - specifically knucklebone dice.</note>. *Those who come to consult the oracle, having performed a prayer to the statue and pronounced their vows, throw four* DICE *from the abundance at hand onto a table. Each* DIE *is inscribed with certain marks: they seek the interpretation of those marks on the tablet, following the correspondence.* Apuleius, concerning the Procession of Isis, says: *The common people, soaked in joy,* <margin>Book 11, Metamorphoses.</margin> *carrying* DICE, *sacred boughs, and little crowns, having kissed the footprints of the goddess—who stood on the steps fashioned of silver—returned down to their own hearths.* Clement of Alexandria, concerning the secret and sacred use not only of the die, but also of the hoop, the spinning top, and the whirligig, where he discusses the mysteries of Bacchus in Eusebius: *It will not be useless* <margin>Book 2, On the Preparation for the Gospel, chapter 3.</margin> *to include here the symbols of the* SECRET RITE, *useless though they may be, so that they may be condemned by the judgment and senses of all. These are: The Die, the Ball, the Spinning Top, Apples, the Whirligig, the Mirror, and the Fleece.* <note>original Greek: "Αστράγαλος, Σφαῖρα, Στρόβιλος, Μῆλα, Ῥόμβος, Ἔσοπτρον, Πόκος."</note>

One may likewise see Anubis snarling, with a netted garment at the breast, the rest covered in fine linen, and with a small bucket <note>a situla</note>, which was carried in the rites of Isis. <margin>GEMMA 54. Book on Ring Gems, ch. 106</margin>

# ON THE ABRAXAS GEMS

The towering neck of ANUBIS <note>The jackal-headed Egyptian god of the dead, often merged with the Greek Hermes/Mercury in this period</note> appears, bearing a CADUCEUS <note>The herald's staff entwined with snakes</note> in his LEFT hand, while shaking a green PALM branch in his RIGHT. Macrobius <note>A 5th-century Roman provincial writer</note> says of the caduceus of this same Mercury: <margin>Saturnalia, Book 1</margin> *In the figure of Mercury,* he says, *it is clear that the SUN IS ALSO WORSHIPPED by the CADUCEUS, which the Egyptians fashioned in the likeness of a male and female dragon joined together to be consecrated to Mercury. These dragons are tied together in the middle part of their coils into a knot, which they call the Knot of Hercules; their foreparts, bent back into a circle, join the perimeter of the circle with pressed kisses; and afterwards the tails are brought back to the handle of the caduceus, and are adorned by wings springing from that same part of the handle. The Egyptians also extend the symbolism of the caduceus to the generation of men, which is called Genesis, mentioning that four gods are present as protectors to a man at birth: Spirit, Fortune, Love, and Necessity <note>original: "δαίμονα, τύχην, ἔρωτα, ἀνάγκην"</note>. And they wish the first two to be understood as the SUN and the MOON, because the SUN, the author of spirit, heat, and light, is the creator and guardian of human life; and therefore he is believed to be the Spirit <note>original: "Dæmon"</note>, that is, the God, of the one being born. The MOON is Fortune <note>original: "τύχη"</note>, because she is the ruler of bodies, which are tossed about by the variety of chance events. Love is signified by the kiss, Necessity by the knot.*

<margin>GEM 55.</margin> Kircher <note>Athanasius Kircher, a famous 17th-century Jesuit scholar</note> also recorded the last gem from Pignorius, and interpreted it thus: *A human figure with two DOG heads, holding a palm in the right hand, surrounded by stars, below which is seen a man as if rising from a tomb. By this nothing else is indicated but ANUBIS, or MERCURY, who, according to Plutarch, is the guardian of both horizons, the lower and the upper; and therefore he is painted with TWO DOG heads. This same figure is described by Diodorus as the leader of souls back from the underworld; hence he carries the palm as a symbol of victory, and a flame, by which it is indicated that souls, having paid their penalties in the underworld, are each soon to be carried into the spheres and stars.* He adds that similar to this amulet is the one we show at number 49, in which Mercury appears as a two-headed Dog-form <note>original: "Κυνόμορφος"</note>, with two figures <margin>GEM 49.</margin>

# COMMENTARY. CHAPTER VIII.

->N 3<-

figures above the two channels of the Nile vessel. But this otherwise most perceptive man did not notice that in the human figure, which he claims has two dog heads, one head is actually human, and only the other is a dog's head emerging from the back of its neck. Furthermore, he offers nothing regarding the knife, or small sword, which is held as if against the throat of the figure rising from the tomb. Therefore, if I may be permitted to write what comes to mind, I will not hide my belief that this gem is nothing other than a certain image of the Mithraic rite, and of the Sun beginning to pass through Aries <note>The Ram, the first sign of the Zodiac</note>; hence the horns sprouting from his forehead. For we have already shown from Macrobius <note>A 5th-century Roman writer known for his work on mythology and the soul</note> that the Sun is worshiped in the form of Mercury—indeed, that Mercury is the same as Apollo. And it seems that Mithras carries not a flame, but a sprinkler <note>original: "aspergillum"</note>, such as is seen among the insignia of the High Priesthood on coins. Both Tertullian, Lampridius, Suidas, and others mention that those about to be initiated into the rites of Mithras were dipped in water; they even report a "mock martyrdom" of these initiates, a redemption of a crown under a sword, and that same crown being offered and then rejected with a sword likewise placed in between. This Gem represents all these things so aptly and so fittingly that it is of great importance to look at it more closely; since nothing could be brought forward to provide greater light to those writers—unless someone were so fastidious as to demand a crown instead of a palm branch, as if both were not customarily used as a symbol of victory, which Mithras commanded his initiated soldiers to expect through that feigned martyrdom. The Emperor Julian <note>Julian the Apostate, who attempted to revive paganism</note> speaks of the Sun, when it enters Aries, carrying souls upward (as seen on the Gem), and of his secret rites of that kind: <margin>Oration 5.</margin> *Because, he says, the SUN draws all things from the earth, because he rouses and gives life to them by a certain warmth and admirable heat, when, as I believe, he brings forth bodies by separating and sifting them into exquisite subtlety;*

<summary>The author interprets an ancient gemstone as a depiction of Mithraic initiation rites and solar symbolism, correcting previous scholars regarding the figure's anatomy and identifying symbols like the sword and palm branch.</summary>

--- Page 148 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of solar symbolism and the ascent of the soul, transitioning from Neoplatonic philosophy to early Christian critiques of mystery cults.</meta>

# ON THE ABRAXAS GEMS

Since the sun raises and lifts up those things which tend downward, we may take this as evidence of its hidden powers. For if he who produces such effects in physical bodies through bodily heat, will he not—through that secret, entirely incorporeal, divine, and pure nature residing in his rays—much more attract and lift up **BLESSED SOULS** to himself? Therefore, since it is established from what has been said so far that this light is kindred to the immortal gods and to those who desire to **RAISE** themselves; and since this same light is accustomed to increase in our world, and the day becomes longer than the night when the **SUN**, as king, begins to pass through **ARIES** <note>The zodiac sign of the Ram, marking the spring equinox and a time of spiritual rebirth</note>: we have simultaneously shown that the rays of this same God possess a certain power of **CARRYING UPWARD**, not only by a manifest effectiveness but also by a hidden one. By this power, indeed, **INNUMERABLE SOULS HAVE BEEN CARRIED ON HIGH**, having followed that sense which is most brilliant and most like the **SUN**. Now, if I wished to pursue those secret and mystical matters which the Chaldean <note>Likely referring to the "Chaldean Oracles," a text of spiritual philosophy used by late Roman mystics</note> celebrated concerning the god distinguished by seven rays, so that through him he might carry **SOULS ON HIGH**, I would be speaking of obscure things, largely unknown to the common people; even though they are well known to those blessed men who practice the **Theurgic** <note>term: Theurgy (divine work); a system of white magic practiced in late antiquity to unite the soul with the divine</note> rites. Therefore, I shall pass over these things in silence for the present. 

<margin>Book on Prescriptions</margin>
Ter-tullian <note>An early Christian author (c. 155–220 AD) known for attacking pagan rituals</note> writes concerning the dipping in water and the feigned martyrdom in such rites: The [devil] himself also **BAPTIZES** <note>original: "tingit"</note> certain ones, namely his believers and faithful; he promises the expiation of sins from a **BATH OF WASHING**, and thus **INITIATES** them into the rites of **MITHRAS** <note>A mystery religion centered on the god Mithras, popular among Roman soldiers</note>; there he signs **HIS SOLDIERS** on their foreheads, celebrates also an offering of bread, introduces an **IMAGE OF THE RESURRECTION**, and redeems a **CROWN** under the 

<margin>Book on the Soldier's Crown</margin>
**SWORD**. The same author says elsewhere regarding the soldier of Mithras: *When he is initiated in the cave—in the camp of darkness, as it were—a crown is offered to him at the point of an **INTERPOSED SWORD** as a kind of mockery of martyrdom; then, when it is placed upon his head, he is instructed to push it off his head with his hand.*

<summary>This page explores the sun's power to elevate souls, referencing Chaldean mysticism and the "seven-rayed god," before quoting Tertullian's critique of the Mithraic mysteries as demonic imitations of Christian sacraments.</summary>
<keywords>Solar theology, Aries, soul's ascent, Theurgy, Chaldean Oracles, Tertullian, Mithras, initiation rites, baptism, Mithraic crown</keywords>

# COMMENTARY. CHAPTER VIII.

<margin>Book 10.</margin>
In a ritual not unlike this, though more solemn, we find in Livy <note>Titus Livius, a Roman historian; this passage refers to the "Samnite Linen Legion" and their terrifying initiation</note>: *The **SOLDIER** was brought to the altars, more like a **SACRIFICIAL VICTIM** than a participant in the rite; and he was bound by an oath not to reveal what he had seen or heard in that place. Then he was forced to swear (with centurions surrounding him with **DRAWN SWORDS**) by a certain dreadful incantation, composed for the cursing of his person and his family, should he not go into battle wherever the Commander led; and if he, or others, fled from the line of battle, or if he saw anyone fleeing and did not immediately kill them.* And in Ammianus Mar-
<margin>Book 21.</margin>
cellinus: *They were all ordered to swear in his [Julian Caesar's] name, with **SWORDS held to their NECKS**, and they swore in formal words under dreadful curses.* From this, my father Jean-Jacques Chifflet <note>original: "Io. Iacobus Chifletius"; a famous 17th-century physician and antiquarian</note> concludes,
<margin>Light 13. Prerogative for the Spanish Claims.</margin>
and shows through many examples, that the custom has descended to us whereby today a sword is touched to the neck of a recruit being initiated into the honor of Knighthood <term>Militiæ: here referring to the knightly class rather than common soldiery</term>, which is commonly called *The Accolade* <note>original: "La Collée"; the ritual blow or touch on the neck/shoulder to dub a knight</note>. From this also, in Spain, those guilty of high treason—as being unworthy of military insignia and stripped of rank—are executed from behind and at the neck; other criminals are struck in the throat from the front, a less infamous death which they call *Beheading* <note>original: "Degollar"</note>.

And so that power is distinguished by a triple form and figure, so that the new moon is represented by a white garment, golden sandals, and **KINDLED TORCHES**: moreover, that **BASKET** <note>original: "calathus," a wicker basket used in religious processions</note> which she herself carries, when she has already been raised to a higher place, is a symbol of the ripeness of the crops, which she brings about by nourishing them around the increase of her **LIGHT**: finally, the bronze color of the sandals is held to be a symbol of the full moon. # COMMENTARY. CHAPTER IX.

<margin>Book 5 of the *Preparation for the Gospel*, chapter 34.</margin>
This is a foul superstition, which Eusebius <note>Eusebius of Caesarea (c. 260–339 AD) was a Christian historian and bishop who wrote extensively against pagan practices.</note> explodes, saying: *What fruit for this life, after all, can men carry away from the deceptions of sorcerers? What could the senseless and dull superstition of empty idols hold that is pleasing to God? Furthermore, of what divine power should images expressed in this kind of configuration be considered representatives? Ought not those authors and exhorters to have pursued the study of worshipful Philosophy, rather than the pursuit of magical and prohibited arts? Especially since a way of life established upon the precepts of virtue and philosophy can be sufficient for living well and blessedly.*

The ancients, blinded by ignorance of the true religion, also worshipped the Monkey, and that tailed creature which they specifically called the *Cercopithecus* <note>From the Greek *kerkos* (tail) and *pithekos* (ape); likely referring to long-tailed monkeys like the guenon or baboon.</note>. Lucian <note>Lucian of Samosata, a 2nd-century satirist known for mocking traditional mythology.</note> says in *The Assembly of the Gods*: *I am ashamed to enumerate the storks, and the* MONKEYS, *and the goats, and things far more absurd than these, which have somehow ascended from Egypt all the way into heaven.* The same author says in *On Sacrifices*: *If you travel to Egypt, there at last, there you will see many things truly religious and plainly worthy of heaven: for instance, Jupiter with the face of a ram; Mercury, that greatest of gods, with the face of a dog; and Pan as a complete goat. In addition to these, one person worships the Ibis, another the Crocodile, and another even the* MONKEY.
Hence Juvenal <note>A Roman satirical poet of the late 1st and early 2nd century AD.</note> writes:

<margin>Satire 15</margin>
> *Who does not know, Volusius of Bithynia, what kind of*
> *monsters demented Egypt worships? One part adores*
> *the Crocodile, another fears the Ibis sated with serpents.*
> *The golden image of a sacred* MONKEY <note>original: "CERCOPITHECI"</note> *shines,*
> *where the magic chords resound from the broken statue of Memnon,*
> *and ancient Thebes lies ruined with her hundred gates.*

And in the works of Prudentius <note>A Roman Christian poet (c. 348–413 AD).</note>, the Roman martyr says:

<margin>Crowns of Martyrdom, Hymn 10.</margin>
> *Do you pray to Venus? Then pray also to the* MONKEY:

# ON THE ABRAXAS GEMS

<margin>Horapollo, *Hieroglyphics*, Book 1, Chapter 14</margin>
HORAPOLLO <note>original: "IAN:" likely referring to Johannes or a specific edition of Horapollo</note>: *The Egyptians,* he says, *when representing letters, paint a BABOON <term>Cynocephalus; literally "dog-head," referring to the sacred baboon associated with Thoth</term>, because there is a certain kinship, a race of BABOONS that knows letters. For this reason, as soon as a BABOON is brought into a sacred temple, the priest places a tablet before him along with a REED PEN and ink: specifically to make a trial of whether he is of that kind of BABOONS who know letters.* Then, concerning the wondrous harmony of that same BABOON with the Moon: *When representing the Moon, they paint a BABOON: because this animal has a certain sympathy with the meeting of the goddess <note>The Moon</note>, by which it is also accustomed to be affected. For when the MOON, meeting with the SUN for some time, remains without light and opaque, then the male BABOON neither looks at anything nor eats: but with his face cast down to the ground in indignation, as if mourning the snatching away of the MOON, he is saddened. The female, truly, never turns her eyes away, and suffers the same as the male. Moreover, wishing to indicate the RISING MOON, they again paint a BABOON in this posture: STANDING, AND LIFTING HIS HANDS TO HEAVEN. They depict the BABOON in this figure to show the MOON’S RISING, because in this posture the BABOON is seen to congratulate the goddess, because both—I mean the SUN and the MOON—are partakers of light.* These things he says, which are certainly of no ambiguous significance for explaining the second Gem, in which both Harpocrates <note>The child Horus, god of silence and the morning sun</note> sitting on a lotus, and a hoopoe with its foldable crest are to be seen—a bird which, according to Albertus Magnus and Evax, is *entirely a bird of illusions and augury.*

It is in the sign of Scorpio, and in the North; it has the nature of Saturn and Mars. Its virtue, if it is carved in stone,*

# CHAPTER X.
## TABLES XV. XVI. XVII.
### ABRAXAS *Serpent-formed* <note>original Greek: Ὀφιόμορφος (Ophiomorphos)</note>.

The OPHITES, says Epiphanius <note>A 4th-century Church Father known for his writings against heresies</note>, <margin>Heresy 37</margin> flourished from Nicolaus and the GNOSTICS, and other earlier sects; they are usually called OPHITES, that is, SERPENT-PEOPLE <note>original Latin: Anguineos</note>, from that SERPENT which they celebrate. But truly horrendous are the things which these men, so to speak, belch forth, filled as if with a foul odor from that consumption. For they worship the SERPENT as if it were some NEW DEITY. The SERPENT proclaims itself to be their CHRIST; or rather, it does not do so itself, for it cannot speak: but the devil, who cast such a thought into their minds, does so. Indeed, who is there who, as soon as he sees a SERPENT, does not recognize an enemy and flee? For this reason, the Lord placed hostile enmities between the SERPENT and the human race, because the devil had used this kind of beast as an instrument and deceived man within the enclosures of paradise; so that those who behold the hostile appearance of the external SERPENT might beware of its snares, and even, so to speak, shudder at the very sight of it. But the OPHITES attribute all knowledge to this creature, and thus they profess...

<summary>This page introduces Chapter X, focusing on the Ophite sect, a Gnostic group that worshipped the serpent as a divine figure or a manifestation of Christ. The author critiques this belief as a diabolical deception, contrasting it with the traditional biblical view of the serpent as man's enemy since the Fall in Paradise.</summary>

# ON THE ABRAXAS GEMS

They claimed that the SERPENT was the author of all knowledge for mankind; from this, they devised MERE FABLES, which they hold as MYSTERIES, though they are nothing more than the ridiculous and empty antics of mimes. Tertullian <note>An early Christian author (c. 155 – c. 220 AD) known for his polemics against heresy.</note> says of these same people: <margin>Book on Prescriptions, chapter 47.</margin> *Those who are called* OPHITES <note>From the Greek 'ophis' meaning serpent; a Gnostic sect that revered the serpent of Genesis.</note> *magnify the SERPENT to such an extent that they even prefer him to* CHRIST *himself: for he, they say, gave us the origin of the knowledge of good and evil. Moses, they claim, noticing his power and majesty, set up the BRAZEN SERPENT, and whoever looked upon it obtained health. Furthermore, they say, CHRIST himself in his Gospel imitates the sacred power of that very SERPENT by saying: "And just as Moses lifted up the SERPENT in the wilderness, so must the Son of Man be lifted up."*

That Taautus <note>An ancient Phoenician deity often identified with the Egyptian Thoth or the Greek Hermes; credited with inventing writing.</note>—the inventor of letters and the author of written commentaries—also perceived snakes to be sprinkled with some portion of divinity; Sanchuniatho <note>A Phoenician author whose works survive only in fragments quoted by later writers.</note> says of him in Eusebius: <margin>Book 1 of the Preparation for the Gospel, chapter 9.</margin> *He attributed a certain DIVINITY to the nature of the DRAGON and of SERPENTS, an opinion which the Phoenicians and Egyptians later confirmed. Indeed, he teaches that this kind of living creature, above all other reptiles, abounds in the force and abundance of spirit, and that its nature is fiery; for this reason, he says, it displays a speed greater than any exception, since it has the support of neither feet, nor hands, nor any other external limb such as other animals are accustomed to use for movement. Furthermore, it displays various and manifold body forms, and twisted in winding coils, it vibrates itself with whatever rapid force it pleases. It is of a very long life, and not only does it grow young again by shedding its old age, but it also receives greater increases in size and strength at the same time, until finally, having completed a certain cycle of years, it is dissolved into itself;*

# COMMENTARY. CHAPTER X.

...just as the same Taautus <note>Also known as Thoth or Hermes Trismegistus</note> left written in the sacred tablets. This was the reason why this genus of living creatures was accustomed to be employed in sacred rites and mysteries alike. Ostanes <note>An ancient Persian magus and alchemist</note> hands down the same things concerning Taautus in the volume he titled the *Octateuch*. Moreover, all the others, adds Eusebius <note>Eusebius of Caesarea, a 4th-century Christian historian</note>, following that leader, philosophized on the nature of things; and having dedicated the first elements in the form of **SERPENTS** with solemn ritual in the temples they had constructed, they celebrated sacrifices, feast days, and Orgies <note>In the classical sense: secret ritual worship</note>; because they thought those were the **GREATEST OF THE GODS**, and the princes and governors of all things. Indeed, among the gods themselves, there was almost no divine power that did not entwine, support, or fortify itself by the omen, necessity, or fellowship of a snake, as the excellent initiate of nature, <margin>Book on the Pythian Viper.</margin> Marcus Antonius Severinus, demonstrated in detail not so long ago. And not only in ancient centuries by the Egyptians, Greeks, and Romans, but also in much more recent times, Jacobus Ouzelius teaches in his observations on Minucius <note>Minucius Felix, an early Christian apologist</note> that divine honors were granted to the serpent by other nations as well. So that one is minded to marvel, along with Pignorius <margin>In the Exposition of the Isiac Table.</margin> <note>Lorenzo Pignoria, an antiquarian who studied the "Isiac Table," a famous bronze tablet with Egyptian motifs</note>, at the stupidity of men who wandered so far from the Best and Greatest God as to worship this foul animal. With such great zeal did the devil labor to assert this symbol of triumphed-over man; I believe, so that he might repair that loss which he had formerly suffered when God commanded the **SERPENT** to be **ACCURSED** among all the living creatures and beasts of the earth.

<margin>Gem 61 and following.</margin> The Gnostics and Basilidians <note>Early Christian sects often labeled as heretics, known for their complex cosmology and use of amulets</note> made this mark of impiety so great that they marked several Gems with its image. That one, which I reported in the second to last place, when I had sent it to our friend Godefroy Wendelin, by order of the Most Serene Archduke of Austria, **LEOPOLD WILLIAM**, who, having recognized and examined the all-encompassing erudition of that best and <margin>Gem 69.</margin> most learn- <note>The text cuts off mid-word: "doctissimi" (most learned)</note>

# ON THE ABRAXAS GEMS

of the most learned old man, he had wished to know his mind concerning the inscription on the front and back of this Chalcedony stone; he responded to me with this opinion:

->DEVOTEE OF LEARNED PIETY,<-
->MOST HONORABLE CHIFLET,<-
->GREETINGS FROM YOUR WENDELIN.<-

The reason I did not respond immediately after receiving your letter eight days ago was quite justified, though tedious (and not without the danger of some slight offense to the Most Serene **Imperial Prince** <note>original: "Augustali Principe"</note>). For while I was looking about quite anxiously for the genuine interpretation of the Gem, and examining everything that the ancient Fathers provide for understanding that era of the Gnostics; I finally (if indeed it is finally) seemed to myself to have carved out this inscribed meaning.

The Serpent biting its own tail <note>The Ouroboros, an ancient symbol of eternity and the cyclical nature of the universe</note>, according to the Hieroglyphics of Horapollo that are in circulation, denotes the World and the Age, and the **King**, and thus even the **King of Kings** himself, **Christ the Lord**; also according to the sense of the holy Fathers, and of **Christ** himself: who clearly taught that he was prefigured by the **Serpent** which Moses lifted up in the desert. And since the world, the age, and the year consist of four parts—East, West, South, and North; and again, Spring, Summer, Autumn, and Winter—and all those are embraced by the number of 365 days through twelve months; therefore, that number four is skillfully represented by the four painted stars. And within the embrace of the serpent itself, the letters **Z Z E** are none other than **T Ξ E** <note>Greek numerals: Tau (300), Xi (60), and Epsilon (5)</note>, or *365*, showing the number of days in the year.

The letters Π ◁ denote *pleroma* <note>original: "πλήρωμα" — a Gnostic term meaning "fullness" or the totality of divine powers</note>, the fulfillment or plenitude of sacred time; which arrived after **Y' E'** <note>Greek numerals for 405</note> 405 years: which are from the 30th year of Artaxerxes (in which they believed the walls of Jerusalem were [rebuilt] by Nehemiah...

<summary>Wendelin provides a symbolic and numerological interpretation of a Gnostic gem, identifying the Ouroboros serpent with Christ and the Greek letters with the 365 days of the solar year.</summary>

# COMMENTARY ON CHAPTER 10

...[that the walls] of Jerusalem had been completed, according to the fourth book of Esdras, chapter 7 <note>The author refers to the apocryphal 2 Esdras (4 Esdras in the Vulgate), often cited in mystical chronologies</note>) up to the death of Christ the Lord. Nor should you wonder that a Z was engraved in place of a T, or a Π < instead of a ΠΔ, or even < for an A. For these and similar variations are seen on coins and inscriptions, and even in the oldest books; besides the fact that the hand of the engraver himself slipped more than once. For instance, where he should have engraved ABRACAZ, he engraved ABRACCE, which makes no sense; or when after the word KAMAPIC he added CC instead of COI; not to mention the solecisms or barbarisms of that era. Having avoided these pitfalls, I read the inscription itself as follows, and thus translate it into the English language: <note>original: "Latinum sermonem" - the author originally translated the Greek into Latin; here it is rendered into English</note>

Α'ζω αὰ, σάω ά, Α. ω. Νεσεμεσεία άμ' Αβρασάξ. Συρράτη ἄκεραμμα Κεραμμά. Καμαρίς σοὶ ἔγλεν β' ἀλφαρὰν γῆς. ἐμεσεία άμ' ωβαήμ. ἀεινάμβων ἄρσ. ἀντὰ Μιχαὴλ, ἄμορα ράχλ φύλα ζέτεμαι ἄνω.

*I lead your things. I save those which belong to the Beginning and the End, I, Nese [the Serpent], the mediatrix along with Abrasax. Pervading things simple and mixed, bowed down to you even unto the second parts of the earth, bringing out at the same time things obscured, Eternal Vertex of happiness, together with Michael, I seek out from on high the races unhappy with calamities.*

The Paraphrastic Sense is:

*I, Christ, the Eternal Wisdom of God, take care of your affairs, as many as they are from the Beginning to the End, O wretched man. I am that Mosaic serpent; <note>A reference to the bronze serpent raised by Moses, interpreted as a type of Christ</note> GRACE, the Mediatrix between God and men; together with ABRASAX [the efficacy of the Holy Trinity and the Passion on the Cross] pervading the most simple natures of the Angels, as well as the masses of men composed of soul and body. I voluntarily bow myself to you, and incline myself descending even to the lower parts of the earth, leading out from there those sitting in darkness and the shadow of death, and bringing them to the eternal heights of happiness. To bestow this upon wretched mortals, through the ministry of Michael the Archangel, I seek out from everywhere men of every kind afflicted by calamities, to be carried up to me on high.*

That A and Ω denote the Beginning and the End is more than well known from the Apocalypse <note>The Book of Revelation</note>.

Νεσε <note>Nese</note> is that Egyptian word Νεσι <note>Nesi</note>; by which word the Egyptians to this day call the five final days of their year which complete the year. This word is certainly the same as the Hebrew נחש NAHAS <note>meaning "Serpent"</note>,

<summary>The author provides a linguistic and theological decoding of a mystical Greek inscription, interpreting it as a voice of Christ acting as a mediator (the "Mosaic Serpent") alongside the Archangel Michael. He explains various orthographic errors in ancient engravings and links Egyptian calendrical terms to Hebrew roots.</summary>

<keywords>Abrasax, Gnosticism, Archangel Michael, Christology, Mosaic Serpent, Greek epigraphy, Egyptian calendar, Nesi, Nahas, Alpha and Omega</keywords>

# ON THE ABRAXAS GEMS

NAHAS, which signifies both a serpent and that curious superstition—or shall I call it the folly—of the Astrologers <note>original: "Genethlicorum," those who cast birth charts</note> who scrutinize years, months, days, and hours: a practice condemned by all wise men, but most explicitly by the Apostle in the 4th chapter of Galatians. From this, therefore, the word <term>Nesemeia</term> <note>Greek: Νεσεμεεία</note> is composed, which quite clearly manifests that serpentine [of Christ] mediating <term>GRACE</term> proceeding from the Cross.

<term>ABRACAX</term> <note>A variant spelling of Abraxas</note> has hitherto been seen as a monstrous name, as if you were naming a demon of the Gnostics. But unless everything deceives me, here is the true context of the initial letters:

| | | | | |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | <term>Ab</term> | <term>אב</term> | Father | 1. |
| B | <term>Ben</term> | <term>בן</term> | Son | 2. |
| R | <term>Ruach</term> | <term>רוח</term> | Spirit | 100. |
| A | <term>Hakadosh</term> | <term>הקדוש</term> | Holy | 1. |
| X | <term>Soteria</term> | | Salvation | 200. |
| A | <term>Apo</term> | | by the Benefit | 1. |
| X | <term>Xylou</term> | | of the Wood <note>The Cross</note> | 60. |
| | | | The sum of the numbers is: | 365. |

Thus, therefore, the mystery of the Trinity has wrought salvation for us from the Wood, when <term>GOD REIGNED FROM THE WOOD</term> <note>A common patristic reference to Psalm 96:10</note>, and He brought that fullness, that <term>pleroma</term> <note>Greek: πλήρωμα, meaning "fullness" or "abundance"</note> of the sacred 365-day period, measured in a full cycle of years, months, and days from the <margin>thirtieth</margin> year of Artaxerxes, when the holy city and the holy temple were restored, until after 405 <term>U.E.</term> <note>likely "Years of the Era"</note> years the Temple of the Body of Christ was destroyed and rebuilt in three days. <term>Abaxem</term> <note>Greek: ἀϐάξημ</note> is clearly an entirely Hebrew word <term>Abim</term> <note>Hebrew: עבים</note> denoting Clouds, Mist, Shadow, or Darkness; namely, those very people sitting in darkness and the shadow of death whom the Key of David led out of the prison house.

I do not here compile all the chests of the Grammarians in which I might wrap every single word: but it would be a matter of conscience to pass over one <term>zeteuma</term> <note>Greek: ζήτευμα, a "search" or "inquiry"</note> instead of <term>zetema</term>, since also from <term>zen</term>, which is *to live*, He is called <term>zoos</term>, the *giver of life*: and also elsewhere the letters <term>eta</term> and <term>epsilon</term> undergo an exchange with one another.

And so much for the present regarding the front of this Gem. The other side exhibits an inscription on the back <note>original: "Opistographum"</note> divided into three small columns: the first of which contains <term>14</term> either small lines or words,

# COMMENTARY. CHAPTER X.

...or the first three letters of the words: the second runs to **14**, the third has **15**.

In these, the name **IAO** <note>original: "IAΩ"; a Greek vocalization of the Hebrew Tetragrammaton</note> is frequent, or with the positions changed to **AIO**, then **AIO** or **OAI** or **IOA** or however else; whoever devised these things wished to vary that and other names by the transposition of letters. You recognize both on this Gem, and on many others, the [superstition] of the Gnostics' father concerning this name **IAO**, and also **AIO** (which is Yahweh <note>original: "יהוה"</note> as it is now written), the <term>ineffable Name of the living God</term>. But you will no doubt ask what those little columns signify, marked with the orders of **13**, **14**, and **15**.

I shall tell you then: these Gnostics sowed the seeds of Astrological study as much as they could; and from them arose the *tessareskaidekatitai* <note>Greek: "τέσσαρες καὶ δεκατίται"</note> or <term>Quartodecimans</term> <note>From the Latin "quartadecima" meaning fourteenth; a group that celebrated Easter on the 14th day of the lunar month</note>, who announce Passover on the fourteenth Moon, and keep their fasts up until that Moon; which fasts they broke on that Moon, on whatever day of the week it fell. For they did not wait for the following Sunday after the manner of the Roman Catholics. And indeed, from the new moon of the month of March found through their own calculations, they counted thirteen days, which they called *pro ton phosteron* <note>Greek: "πρὸ τῶν φωστήρων", meaning "before the luminaries"</note> (repeatedly mentioned in the Alexandrian Chronicle), which they wanted to be passed religiously before they began to count the **14th** day at evening. But once the **14th** day had begun at evening, they did not immediately begin their Passover; rather, they consecrated that day as the Vigil of Passover, and devoted themselves to sacred readings that whole night and the following day, until that **14th** day was concluded at evening. Thus, with the Vigil of Passover now closed, and the **14th** day having ended at evening, they broke the fast at the start of the **15th** and began the Paschal joy. This reasoning, therefore, made those three numbers **13**, **14**, and **15** sacred and holy to them; upon which there is a wide field for discussion, and material for a proper Commentary, and one worthy of an **August Prince**, if only I were so worthy and so fit to handle it; meanwhile ceasing nothing which I have professed in this writing: and which (if you approve) you will offer in my name to the Most Serene [Prince]. After greeting a thousand and a thousand times your parent and mine, and the Archimandrite of Balerne; continue to love him whom (I promise) you cannot hate.

->Yours with full devotion, **Gottfried Wendelin**<-
->*Canon of Tournai, the day before the Ides of September, 1655.*<-

<summary>Gottfried Wendelin explains the mystical significance of the numbers 13, 14, and 15 on Gnostic gems, linking them to the Quartodeciman practice of calculating Easter (Pascha) based on the lunar calendar. The page concludes with a formal dedication and sign-off to his correspondent.</summary>

# ON THE ABRAXAS GEMS

Our friend Wendelinus wrote these things to me, through which he attempted to find gold in the mud—a pearl among the Basilidian swine <note>The Basilidians were a 2nd-century Gnostic sect founded by Basilides; the author views their beliefs as "mud" or "swine-like" compared to orthodox Christianity</note>. He believed their trifles amounted to something significant, and with a pious commentary, he most ingeniously transformed them into very serious and most religious rites. For, to pass over other matters where the authority of such a great man carries much weight with me, the ancient Christians—in order to separate themselves from the customs of such heretics even in this form of worship—were accustomed to represent Christ not as a serpent, but as a dove. This seems to be the point of what is found in Tertullian <note>An early Christian author from Carthage, c. 155–220 AD</note>:

<margin>Book against the Valentinians, chapters 2 and 3.</margin>
> *In short, a <term>DOVE</term> <note>original: COLVMBA</note> has been accustomed to <term>DEMONSTRATE CHRIST</term>, whereas the <term>SERPENT</term> <note>original: SERPENS</note> is accustomed to <term>TEMPT</term> him. The former, from the beginning, was the herald of divine peace; the latter, from the beginning, was the plunderer of the divine image. Thus, simplicity alone will more easily be able both to recognize and to reveal God; cleverness alone is more likely to shatter and betray. Therefore, let the <term>SERPENT</term> hide himself as much as he can, and let him twist all his cleverness in the windings of his hiding places; let him live deep down, let him be thrust into dark places, let him unroll his length through many a turn, let him move tortuously, never showing his whole self at once—a beast that flees the light. The home of <term>OUR DOVE</term> is simple, always in high places, and open, and facing the light: she loves the figure of the Holy Spirit, the rising figure of <term>CHRIST</term>.*

<margin>Chapter 2. Book 3 of The Instructor, chapter 11.</margin>
Hence the same Tertullian says: We are marked as <term>SIMPLE ONES</term> <note>original: SIMPLICES</note> among them. This is because, as noted by Clement of Alexandria (a contemporary of Tertullian), Christians were accustomed to carry on their signet rings not a serpent, but a dove, or a fish, or something else of that kind, by which a sacred matter might be hinted at. Cyprian <note>Bishop of Carthage, d. 258 AD</note> writes in *On the Unity of the Church*: *This is the <term>SIMPLICITY</term> of the <term>CHURCH</term> that must be recognized, this is the charity to be maintained, so that the love of the brotherhood may imitate <term>DOVES</term>.* Paulinus <note>Paulinus of Nola, c. 354–431 AD</note> writes concerning the Basilica of Saint Felix:

> *And the <term>DOVES</term> which sit above the celestial sign,*
> *Reveal that the kingdoms of God lie open to the <term>SIMPLE</term>.*

<summary>The author contrasts the "serpent" symbolism of Gnostic sects like the Basilidians with the "dove" and "fish" symbols used by early orthodox Christians to represent Christ and simplicity. He cites early Church Fathers like Tertullian and Clement of Alexandria to argue that true Christian faith is characterized by openness and light rather than the secretive, twisting nature of heresy.</summary>

# COMMENTARY. CHAPTER X.

And again in the same Temple:

> *You will also make us into DOVES pleasing to you, O Christ,*
> *If your portion flourishes within our pure hearts.*

<margin>John 3.</margin>
The Fathers certainly acknowledge, and Christ himself taught, that he was prefigured by the serpent <note>A reference to the Bronze Serpent in the wilderness, often used as a symbol for Christ's crucifixion</note>, but in a different sense than what fits the fables of the Gnostics: for those IMAGES and SIGNS,
<margin>Heresy 37</margin>
says Epiphanius, *which God excellently transferred toward heavenly doctrine, they impudently adapted to assert the FALSEHOOD of their own* DOGMAS. *Inasmuch as* JESUS *Christ our Lord, and the same God the Word, begotten by the Father before all ages without any beginning or time,* IS BY NO MEANS *a* SERPENT. *Far be it from us to think so; rather, he came against the* SERPENT.

Now, where the most illustrious Wendelinus <note>Godefroy Wendelin (1584–1667), a Flemish astronomer and priest</note> claims to have found the context of the initial letters of the name ABRACAE, he unearths from them the highest mysteries of the Christian Religion—namely, the Trinity and the salvation wrought for us by Christ from the Wood <note>The Cross</note>. From these views, the Fathers differ wonderfully in their interpretation of the same name; among whom Jerome is so clear that nothing could be more so:
<margin>On the 1st chapter of Obadiah.</margin>
*Is not,* he says, *the thought of* BASILIDES <note>An early Gnostic teacher in Alexandria</note> *impudent and AGAINST GOD, and the portentous name* ABRAXAS, *which is* PREFERRED *over the Lord the Creator?* Indeed (which is diametrically opposed to Wendelinus's opinion), Basilides denied that Christ suffered or was crucified, and he forbade men to suffer martyrdom for the sake of His Name,
<margin>Tertullian, On Prescriptions, chapter 46.</margin>
*who had neither suffered nor been crucified.* Hence, a man of fair judgment and most exquisite discernment, Vopiscus Fortunatus Plempius <note>A noted 17th-century physician and scholar</note>, wrote to me: *That he had been not moderately delighted by the letter of our friend Wendelinus; indeed, he was stunned by his interpretation. That man is a marvel, who could carve such a sacred meaning out of a thing of nothing; and in this too it is a wonder, that*
<margin>Eusebius book 4 of Ecclesiastical History, chapter 8. Augustine, Philastrius, and others on Heresies.</margin>

# ON THE ABRAXAS GEMS

...because he made the word **ABRACAE** neither *Hebrew* nor *Greek*, but a *hybrid*. I certainly maintain, among the other praises of this most friendly man, that this is not the least: that many things are found in his writings that are not of common flavor, and which can be defended by no one other than himself. However, since in this kind of literature one errs with very little danger, I will not be deterred from stating here what I think about the unique expression **CEMECEIA**. For one must engage in some divination <note>The author uses "divination" here to mean scholarly guesswork or intuitive interpretation of obscure texts.</note> and turn toward the **SUN**, which was so familiar to the Basilidians <note>A Gnostic sect founded by Basilides in the 2nd century AD, known for their complex cosmology and use of amulets.</note>, and to which the inscription itself leads us, as if it were a speech added to the Gem. *Shemesh* <note>original: שמש</note> is the **SUN** to the *Hebrews*; *Yah* <note>original: יה</note> is one of the ten names of God, which signifies *Lord*. This name was bestowed, as some prefer, from **BEING**, because God grants **BEING** to all things. From this, unless I am mistaken, comes *Shemesh-Yah* <note>original: שמשיה</note> or **CEMECEIA** <note>The author is suggesting the magical word is a combination of the Hebrew words for Sun and God, meaning "Sun of God".</note>.

<margin>
*Above, chapter 1.*
</margin>

# ON THE ABRAXAS GEMS

...of a woman with a radiated head, the rest being a serpent; others were shown in various other monstrous forms and appearances. They took care to have the figures of these beings carved into the gemstones of rings so that they might serve as amulets. The Greeks say: <note>original: "Εἰσὶν ἐν ἑκάστῳ τῶν ζωδίων τρεῖς κατειλεγμένοι Δεκανοὶ..."</note> *In each of the signs of the zodiac there are three appointed* **DECANS**, <term>Decans: astrological deities who rule over ten-degree segments of the zodiac</term> *varied in form, of whom one holds an axe, while another is depicted in some other likeness. If you carve their forms and appearances into the* **BEZELS OF RINGS**, *they will serve as* **AMULETS AGAINST ADVERSITIES**; *so says Teucer <note>Teucer of Babylon, an influential ancient astrologer</note> and others of his time who excelled in the study of the heavens.* Even today, among the <term>heirlooms</term> <note>original: "κειμήλια"</note> of the curious, many rings are found—or stones shaped to be hung around the neck—upon which various **figures** <note>original: "σχήματα"</note> and names of the Decans remain carved and inscribed, most often with radiated heads. Such is the one of which a friend provided a **cast** <note>original: "ἔκτυπον"</note>, showing the radiated and maned face of a lion ending in the coiled folds of a serpent, with the name **Chnoubis**. That same friend who sent it thought it should be read as **Anubis**, as if the engraver had mistakenly written a 'Ch' instead of an 'A'. But I advised him that this is the name of a Decan in Cancer, who is called **Chnoumis** by Hephaestion. <note>Hephaestion of Thebes, a 4th-century astrologer</note> Nothing is more common among the Greeks than for 'm' and 'b' to be exchanged for one another. However, the true name is **Chrysis**. For *chrysos* is "gold" in the Egyptian language. <note>The author is likely conflating Greek "chrysos" with an Egyptian root</note> Whence the proper name *Chrysis* is the same as *Chryses* to the Greeks. It seems the name *Chrysis* was given to this Decan because he was of a golden and tawny color, like a lion's mane. For he does not seem to be so named because he grants riches. On the contrary, one born while this Decan is the <term>Horoscope</term> <note>The "Horoscopus" refers to the rising sign or degree at birth</note> will squander his paternal inheritance.] These are the words of Salmasius. <note>Claude Saumaise, a famous 17th-century French classical scholar</note> But Kircher <note>Athanasius Kircher, the Jesuit polymath</note> reads and explains a similar gem differently,

73.

he posits; I was amazed by his explanation of the <term>Gemma</term> <note>Latin: "gem" or "engraved stone"</note> which appears in the works of Pignorius, and which I present again among others as number 74. <margin>74. In the second part of volume 2 of the *Egyptian Oedipus*, class 11, chapter 6.</margin> *The serpent, he says, or asp, is coiled in a circle; its head, surrounded by a radiant halo, reveals a Mercurial deity, as appears from the inverted letters where one reads ANUBIS <note>original Greek: ΑΝΟΥΒΕΙ</note>. CHNOUMISRI, however, is the name which, when resolved into numbers, makes 1480; and the name CHRIST <note>original Greek: ΧΡΙΣΤΟΣ</note> makes the same amount.*

<note>This table demonstrates "isopsephy," a practice where the numerical values of letters in different words are summed to find hidden connections. Here, the author compares the Greek name for Christ with a Gnostic deity name.</note>

*You see, therefore, how perversely and how impiously the heretical magicians profane the divine name of Christ with barbarian names of this sort.* So he says. And yet, on the Gem, CHNOUBIS or CHNOUMIS is not represented as a serpent coiled in a circle, but as lion-headed <note>original Latin: "leontomorphos"</note> and well-maned; nor is it inscribed CHNOUMISRI, but CHNOUMIS, as is evident from the examples cited. Concerning these matters, I would wish judgment to be passed by others, and especially by Kircher himself: for my nature and upbringing make me reluctant to criticize the works of others, preferring instead to submit my own to censorship.

<meta>This page continues the discussion of Gnostic gems and astrological decans, specifically focusing on the iconography of the lion-headed serpent and its connection to the zodiac.</meta>

# ON THE ABRAXAS GEMS

<margin>Book on the Climacteric Years</margin> On the first lion was written. This is, he says, *Cholchnubis* <note>original: "χολχνουβίς"</note>—called *Charchnumis* by Hephaestion—possessing a human face with seven rays and the tail of a scorpion. Beneath its chin, the sign of Sagittarius was inscribed, which is crossed by a small rod forked at both ends. I do not know why: for that Decan <note>A "Decan" is a deity presiding over ten degrees of the zodiac</note> has nothing in common with Sagittarius. It is the first Decan of Leo. However, the Twelfth-part of Leo is ruled by three Decans under the names of Aries, Taurus, and Leo, as is evident from these lines of Manilius:

> *But the Lion remembers the law of its shared Triangle,*
> *And receives the Wool-bearing leader <note>Aries</note>, and the Bull*
> *Joined to itself in a square; the third part is borne under the Twins.*

Did he wish to signify that this stone was engraved while the sign of Sagittarius was rising on the horizon? And yet, it would be more appropriate for it to be dedicated to the "Rising Lion" <note>original: "Ὠρεγονοποιοῦντι λέοντι"</note>, of which Cholchnubis is the Decan. I think, therefore, that the sign of Sagittarius is marked by that figure because it is in "diameter" <note>directly opposite on the zodiac wheel</note> with that Decan. And indeed, so it is. For Sagittarius is six signs away from Cancer, of which Cholchnubis is considered the third Decan. This diameter is by degree, not by sign. Certain Egyptian words written in Greek are seen on it, as well as the name *IAO* <note>A Greek transcription of the Hebrew Tetragrammaton, or Yahweh</note>. For that inverted *MU* (𐅽) is placed for an *Omega*, making it *IAO*; the *Sigma* can also be seen as overturned. Furthermore, from this word *IAO*, it appears that these talismanic inscriptions belong to the Basilidians <note>Followers of the 2nd-century Gnostic teacher Basilides</note>, engraved for the observation of the stars along with the figures of the Decans. For they are composed of Egyptian words, and Basilides himself and his followers were from parts of Egypt. The Egyptian inscription itself, except for that one word, is *GIGANTOPANTOREKTA*, which is to say, "The Overthrower of all Giants." It is incorrectly engraved on the stone as *GIGANTOPONTOREKTA*. *Phossein* <note>original: "ῥήσσειν"</note> means to break or to dash to the ground, a term derived from the wrestling floor. Thus far Salmasius <note>Claude Saumaise, a famous 17th-century French classical scholar</note>.

### COMMENTARY. CHAPTER XI.

<margin>GEMS 77. & 78.</margin>
On two other gems, the Father of All <note>original: "Πατὴρ τῶν ὅλων"</note> appears to be engraved in the image of a "Terminus" <note>A boundary stone, often depicted as a pillar-like bust without arms or legs, symbolizing stability</note>, with his arms crossed over his chest, *in the greatest and deepest rest, in the most peaceful leisure of a*, so to speak, *stunned divinity, such as Epicurus commanded:* <note>Referring to the Epicurean view of gods who live in total indifference to human affairs</note> This is further indicated by the subject Angels standing upon celestial spheres with bent knees and hands likewise crossed in a posture of adoration. Regarding these, Epiphanius writes when discussing Simon Magus: <margin>Tertullian, Book on the Valentinians, ch. 7.</margin> *He devised certain names of* PRINCIPALITIES *and* POWERS <margin>Heresy 21</margin> *and placed various* CELESTIAL SPHERES *and certain* INTELLIGENCES *in the firmament and in every heaven, to which he attached* BARBARIC NAMES. *He furthermore denies that any man can achieve salvation unless he has learned such* MYSTERIES *and offered those sacrifices to the* PARENT OF THE UNIVERSE *through those* PRINCIPALITIES *and* POWERS *we have mentioned.* The same author says of Menander: *He, just like the other, asserts that the* WORLD *was fabricated by* ANGELS, *but that he himself is the* POWER OF GOD *sent from heaven.* Then, regarding Saturnilus, a fellow student of Basilides: *Just as Menander did,* <margin>Heresy 22</margin> <margin>Heresy 23</margin> *he preached that the* WORLD *was made by* ANGELS. *Furthermore, that there is* ONE UNKNOWN FATHER, *who created the* VIRTUES, PRINCIPALITIES, *and* POWERS. Finally, regarding Basilides: *There was, says Basilides,* ONE UNBEGOTTEN PRINCIPLE, *who alone is the* FATHER OF ALL: *from him was procreated* <margin>Heresy 24</margin> *Mind <note>original: "Mens"</note>, from Mind the Word; from this Prudence, from which Virtue and Wisdom came forth. From both of these, furthermore,* PRINCIPALITIES, POWERS, *and* ANGELS. *Then these Virtues and* ANGELS *constructed the* FIRST and SUPREME HEAVEN, *and in turn propagated other angels.*

<margin>GEMS 78.</margin>
The last stone of this table is a Heliotrope <note>A variety of chalcedony, also known as bloodstone</note>. The necromancers of the Babylonians call it a "Gem," about which Pliny says: *It is produced in Ethiopia, Africa, and Cyprus, of a leek-green color,* <margin>Book 37, chapter 10.</margin>

--- Page 170 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of Gnostic gems and the spread of heretical doctrines from Egypt into Europe, specifically focusing on the influence of Basilides and the Priscillianists.</meta>

-># ON THE ABRAXAS GEMS<-

<note>original: "ris, sanguineis venis distincta"</note> ...marked with blood-red veins. The reason for its name <note>The Heliotrope or "bloodstone"</note> is that when placed in a vessel of water, it changes the brightness of the sun's reflection into a blood-red color by its impact; the Ethiopian variety is most effective. Outside of the water, this same stone receives the sun like a mirror and detects eclipses, showing the moon passing beneath. The shamelessness of the Magi <note>Ancient Persian priests or magicians</note> is most evident in this example, for they claim that if the herb Heliotrope is mixed with it and certain prayers are added, the person wearing it becomes invisible.

The superstitious doctrine of the Basilidians <note>Followers of the Gnostic teacher Basilides</note> regarding the Decans <note>Thirty-six stars or groups of stars used in ancient Egyptian astronomy to divide the zodiac</note> and celestial signs also infected the Priscillianists themselves. Indeed, during the reign of Emperor Hadrian, as we have mentioned before, Basilides introduced the Gnostic sect into Egypt, and <margin>Juan de Mariana, Book 14, Chapter 5</margin> from his school, Marcus set out for Spain and sowed the seeds of error, drawing the noblewoman Agape and Helvidius to his side. From there, after some time, Priscillian stirred up a great fire from the sparks of a dormant flame. He <margin>Brouwer, Annals of Trier, Book 4</margin> was the Bishop of Avila in Spain, a man of noble birth, restless, and very quick in debate; he believed that humans were bound to one another by a fatal connection to the stars, and that the human body itself was organized according to the proportions of the twelve signs of the Zodiac. He held that one must abstain from fatty or "unclean" foods. And because he attributed the creation of the flesh not to the good and true God, but to malignant angels, he considered it right to separate men from willing wives and wives from their husbands. Indeed, this foul plague grew so strong through the cleverness of the master that the itch for novelty soon seized and spread through people of every sex and rank. For this reason, in the Council of Braga <margin>Canon 9</margin>, it was thus defined by the Fathers: *If anyone believes that human souls and bodies are bound by a fatal sign, as the Pagans and PRISCILLIAN have said, let him be anathema <note>Excommunicated or cursed</note>.* And: <margin>Canon 10</margin> *If any believe that the TWELVE SIGNS, which is to say the signs that the Astrologers <term>Mathematici: in this period, the term referred to astrologers or those who cast horoscopes</term> are accustomed to observe, are distributed through the individual members of the soul or body...*

<summary>This page describes the magical properties of the Heliotrope stone and traces the historical spread of Gnostic and Priscillianist heresies from Egypt to Spain, specifically their belief in astrological fatalism.</summary>
<keywords>Heliotrope, bloodstone, Basilidians, Priscillianism, Gnosticism, Zodiac, astrology, Council of Braga, Spain, Church history</keywords>

They call these <term>Apotelesmata glypheos</term> <note>original Greek: Ἀποτελέσματα γλύψεως</note>, or the *Effects of engraving and the seal*. # COMMENTARY. CHAPTER XII.

He is worshipped; indeed, the Egyptians venerate him with the most sacred and majestic devotion, and they worship him as one lacking a beginning, reaching back beyond their memory, which is the longest of all. And in truth, it is clear from his very name that HERCULES is the SUN. For what else is HERACLES <note>original: "HERACLES"</note> but the GLORY OF THE AIR <note>original: "HERAS, id est AERIS CLEOS"; a common but unscientific ancient etymology linking Hera (air) and Kleos (glory)</note>? Furthermore, what other glory of the AIR is there, if not the illumination of the SUN, by whose withdrawal into the depths the spirit of darkness is hidden? Moreover, the administration of sacred rites among the Egyptians, through manifold actions, asserts the manifold power of God, significantly indicating that this HERCULES is the *Sun who is in all things and through all things* <note>original: "τὸν ἐν πᾶσι & διὰ πάντων Ἥλιον"</note>.

<margin>GEMS 89. & 90.</margin>
They believed that the image of him standing upright and suffocating the lion, engraved upon a gem, was effective against colicky pains. Alexander Trallianus <note>A 6th-century Greek physician</note>, in his work *On Natural Medicines* which are usually bound as amulets for this disease, writes: *Engrave* HERCULES *standing upright, SUFFOCATING the lion, upon a MEDICAL STONE, and give it to be worn set in a GOLDEN RING.* Hence the triple letter Kappa <note>The letter 'K' repeated three times, likely a magical formula</note> on the back of the Gem. Gorlæus <note>Abraham Gorlaeus, a famous collector of engraved gems</note> says of this Amulet: *Trallianus, a physician of no small reputation, relates that the image of* HERCULES *lying on his back and strangling the lion, enclosed in a ring and worn on the finger, is a specific amulet for colic pain.* But this is not quite right: for Trallianus expressly advises that HERCULES should be engraved *upright* <note>original: "Ἡρακλέα ὀρθὸν"</note>, as he appears in the cited Gems, and not *lying on his back*.

<margin>Book 10, chapter 1.</margin>
<margin>Preface to the Dactyliotheca <note>A collection of finger-rings or gems</note>.</margin>

# CHAPTER XIII.
## PLATE XXIII.
### ABRAXAS *The Soldier, Perseus.*

In my opinion, the most learned Johannes Wowerius <note>A 17th-century scholar and philologist.</note> rightly advises in his notes on those words of Firmicus <note>Firmicus Maternus, a 4th-century Christian author who wrote against pagan religions.</note>: *I know, you think this worthy of a Roman name, and of the rites of the Persians. But let it follow by the laws of the Persians... that she is consecrated armed with shield, breastplate, sword, and spear.* Perhaps it should be read: *that he is armed*; and understood as the soldier, who is accustomed to be initiated into the rites of Mithras <note>Mithras was a Persian sun god whose mystery cult was highly popular among Roman soldiers.</note>. But Jerome <note>St. Jerome, the church father.</note> lists the soldier himself among the monstrous images, four of which we exhibit here. The first has a <term>crane-shaped</term> <note>original: "γερανόμορφος" (geranomorphos). Referring to a figure with the head or form of a crane.</note> head, with snakes in the right and left hands. <margin>GEM 94.</margin>

<summary>This page critiques tree-worship through the poetry of Sedulius and begins a scholarly examination of "Abraxas" gems, linking their soldier-like imagery to the Persian mysteries of Mithras.</summary>
<keywords>Sedulius, idolatry, Medusa, Abraxas, Mithras, Johannes Wowerius, Firmicus Maternus, crane-headed figures, Gnosticism</keywords>

# ON THE ABRAXAS GEMS

<margin>GEMM 95.</margin> displays upright serpents; another shows the usual rooster-headed monster <margin>GEMM 96.</margin> of Abraxas; a third stands upon a lotus flower, with a lion nearby <margin>GEMM 97.</margin> reared up on its feet as if leaping; a fourth is represented above with the usual Abraxas monster, holding a spear in the right hand and a globe in the left. And these seem to be the very "soldiers" mentioned by Jerome <note>St. Jerome, a 4th-century Church Father who wrote against heresies.</note>; furthermore, Epiphanius says regarding the Heresy of the Gnostics: *The same people,* he says, *are called <term>Stratiotikoi</term> <note>original Greek: Στρατιωτικοὶ</note>, or* MILITARY *men in Egypt.*

However, as for what Macarius <note>Jean L'Heureux, a 16th-century antiquarian who used the pseudonym Macarius.</note> says—that a man he saw equipped in the manner of a soldier with a sword, a lion's head, and holding the head of Medusa in his right hand, should be called an *Abraxas soldier*—this does not seem entirely certain. I refer this figure to *Perseus*, whom Jerome also mentions among the portentous images of Abraxas; for it was he who beheaded Medusa, and <margin>George the Monk.</margin> *taught the Persians themselves those accursed ceremonies, and* MAGIC, *which is called the magic of* MEDUSA; *during whose reign fire fell from heaven among the Persians, from which he lit another fire, and building a shrine, he placed it therein, and called it the Temple of the* IMMORTAL FIRE, *and appointing wise men to serve and guard the fire, he called them Magi. The Persians, holding these rites in great esteem, worship* FIRE. This should certainly be referred to the ceremonies of Mithras <note>An ancient Persian sun god whose mystery cult was popular in the Roman Empire.</note>; hence the lion's head given to Perseus on the Gem that Macarius saw. In Eusebius <margin>Book 2 of the Preparation, chapter 1.</margin> <note>Eusebius of Caesarea, a 4th-century historian of the early Church.</note>: *They say that the most famous of the Greeks claimed the Heroes and gods of the Egyptians as their own. For even Hercules, though he was Egyptian by race and endowed with singular bravery, traveled over a great part of the world; yet the Greeks proclaim him as if he were Greek, even though he is far different from that other Hercules whom Alcmena bore in Greece many centuries later. They add that* PERSEUS *also first saw the light of day in Egypt; and that the origin of Isis herself was attributed by Greek men...*

<summary>This page discusses the "Military" classification of Gnostic gems, debating whether certain figures represent the deity Abraxas or the hero Perseus, while linking these images to Persian fire-worship and Egyptian origins.</summary>

# CHAPTER XIV.
## TABLE XXIV.
### ABRAXAS The Beetle. <note>original: "Κάνθαρος" (Kantharos). While this can mean a drinking cup, in this context it refers to the Scarab beetle, often associated with the Egyptian god Khepri.</note>

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut initial 'F' featuring floral and scrollwork patterns.</image-desc>
Without a doubt, that gem which Macarius <note>Jean L’Heureux, a 16th-century archeologist who wrote under the name Macarius.</note> saw was an Abraxas Beetle, in which, *on one side, he says, was a certain insect, perhaps a scarab, and around it a serpent in a circle catching its tail in its mouth.* <note>The Ouroboros, an ancient symbol of eternity and the cyclical nature of the universe.</note>

<margin>In the Explanation of the Isiac Table. Gem 98.</margin>
For Pignorius <note>Lorenzo Pignoria (1571–1631), an Italian antiquarian.</note> was the first to publish a carving of a similar gem; and in it, as you see, the head of the Sun is skillfully placed upon a scarab; all of which a serpent devouring its tail encircles, so that it may be understood that the light of this world comes from the Sun, which governs the life of living creatures with its wise course. And indeed, if we listen to Porphyry <note>A Neoplatonic philosopher (c. 234–305 AD).</note>, the Sun is not without reason represented as beetle-shaped <note>original: "Κανθαρόμορφος" (Kantharomorphos)</note>, and conversely, the scarab is represented as sun-headed <note>original: "ἡλιοκέφαλος" (heliokephalos)</note>. For he says: *A foolish person might detest the SCARAB, being a stranger and foreigner to divine matters. But the Egyptians are accustomed to worship it as a living and breathing* IMAGE OF THE SUN. *For every* SCARAB *is male, and having cast its seed into a marshy place and shaped it into a spherical form, it rolls it backward with its hind feet, emulating the motion of the SUN in the sky, and waits for the completion of the Moon's cycle of days.* Arnobius <note>An Early Christian apologist (died c. 330 AD).</note> says regarding the superstitions of the Egyptians: *Temples are built high for cats,* SCARABS, *and young cows.*

<margin>In Eusebius, book 3 of the Preparation for the Gospel, chapter 4.</margin>
<margin>Book 1, Against the Heathen.</margin>

It cites classical and early Christian authors like Porphyry and Arnobius to explain why this insect was considered a divine image.</summary>

# ON ABRAXAS GEMS

<margin>Pliny, Natural History, Book 30, Chapter 11.</margin>
...are raised to great heights. The powers of the gods, though mocked, remain silent; nor are they affected by any resentment because they see their own holiness compared to that of lowly creatures. Indeed, Pliny writes in the same vein: "A great part of Egypt worships **SCARABS** among the **DIVINITIES**," <note>original: "SCARABÆOS inter NVMINA colit"</note> based on the careful interpretation of Apion, who concludes that this animal bears a resemblance to the works of the **SUN**, in order to excuse the rituals of his own nation. 

<margin>Diogenes Laërtius, Lives of the Philosophers.</margin>
Finally, in the writings of Diogenes Laërtius, it is noted that the **SUN** and **MOON** are gods, the former called *Osiris* and the latter *Isis*; Manetho, in his *Epitome of Natural Philosophy*, and Hecataeus, in the first book of *On the Philosophy of the Egyptians*, are authorities for the claim that they represent these gods through the **SCARAB**, the dragon, and other animals. 

<margin>GEM 99.</margin>
Hence, on another Gem, a lightning bolt is placed beneath a scarab, because the beetle was surely not only a symbol of the Sun, but also of the Moon, which has a wonderful sympathy with the waters. These waters, as Pliny elegantly puts it, 

<margin>Book 31, Chapter 1.</margin>
"*Climb into the heights, and even lay claim to the heavens for themselves, and by the covering of clouds, they stifle the vital Spirit. This cause* **STRIKES FORTH LIGHTNING**, 

<margin>Book 2, Chapter 38.</margin>
*while the world itself is at discord with itself. From this also come hail, frost, rains, storms, whirlwinds, and many of the evils of mortals.*" To 

<margin>Book 37, Chapter 9.</margin>
avert these things, the Magi—according to the same witness, Pliny—"*not without contempt and mockery of the human race*," used to **carve EAGLES** and **SCARABS** on **EMERALDS**. 

<margin>GEMS 100 & 101.</margin>
He says elsewhere: "*There are also Chelonitides <note>A mythical 'turtle-stone' believed to be found in the heads of tortoises.</note> resembling tortoises, from which they prophesy many things* **REGARDING STORMS**. *Indeed, that variety which has golden spots, if thrown into boiling water with a* **SCARAB**, *causes* **STORMS** *to be averted.*"

<margin>Book 37, Chapter 10.</margin>

<margin>GEM 102.</margin>
Lastly, as Macarius <note>Jean L'Heureux, a 17th-century antiquarian who wrote under the name Macarius.</note> noted, nothing is more frequent than scarabs fashioned from the solid body of a Gem, which were worn perforated <note>i.e., drilled through so they could be strung as beads or rings.</note>. Many of this kind can be seen in the Treasury of Antiquities of the Most Serene Archduke **LEOPOLD WILLIAM**; and 

<margin>In The Resurrection of Childeric, Chapter 18.</margin>
Jean-Jacques Chifflet, my father, previously displayed twenty of them, each distinguished by its own emblems engraved on the back.

# CHAPTER XV.

### TABLES XXV. XXVI. XXVII.

#### Canopus, Serapis, Isis, Anubis, the Gods of the Egyptians and Gnostics brought to Rome.

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut initial 'S' featuring floral motifs and foliage.</image-desc>

It remains for me to fulfill my promise to briefly weave together the commonalities between the Egyptian or Gnostic superstitions and those of the Romans:

<margin>Prudentius, Book 2 against Symmachus.</margin>
<note>Prudentius was a 4th-century Christian poet; Symmachus was a Roman senator who defended traditional paganism.</note>
> *For Isis and* SERAPIS, *and the* APE *with the great tail,*
> *And the* CROCODILE *are the same as* JUNO, LAVERNA, <note>Goddess of thieves</note> and PRIAPUS.
> *These gods you, O* NILE, *worship, and you, O* TIBER, *adore:*
> *The Latin gods and the Egyptian gods,*
<margin>The same author, Crowns of Martyrdom, Hymn 10.</margin>
> *To whom* ROME *makes sacrifice, and to whom* CANOPUS <note>A city in Egypt, here representing Egyptian religion</note> *offers prayers.*

Hence Lucan writes:
<margin>Book 8.</margin>
> *We have accepted your* ISIS *into* ROMAN *temples,*
> *And the* DOG-HEADED <note>Referring to Anubis</note> *half-gods, and the sistrums* <note>A metallic rattle used in the worship of Isis</note> *commanding grief,*
> *And* OSIRIS, *whom you prove to be a man by your mourning.* <note>Refers to the myth of Osiris's death and resurrection</note>

And indeed, it was not at all surprising that the Egyptian gods were joined with the Latin ones, as many similarities were found between them: *There was one superstition, though the error was not of the same color*, as Prudentius puts it. But near the beginning of the Pontificate of Pope Anicetus <note>Pope from c. 155 to 166 AD</note>, certain most infamous heretics, having found an opportunity during a pressing persecution, forced themselves into the City; so that, if they were able by any means, they might undermine the purity of the most noble Apostolic Church of Christ. Among others who presumed to do this was a bold woman named Marcellina, a follower of
<margin>Book 2 against Symmachus.</margin>
<margin>Baronius, Year of Christ 167, no. 4.</margin>
<note>Cardinal Baronius was a 16th-century historian; this refers to his "Annales Ecclesiastici".</note>

<margin>Heresy 27; Irenaeus, Book 1, Chapter 24.</margin>The mother of the most nefarious sect of the Gnostics <note>A diverse group of early Christian-era religious movements that claimed salvation through secret knowledge (gnosis).</note>, of whom Epiphanius writes: *In the time of Anicetus, MARCELLINA came to Rome, vomiting the poison of the Carpocratian doctrine; she drove many there, corrupted by her error, into destruction: and from that very source the beginnings of the GNOSTICS were stirred up.*

<margin>GEMS 103 & 104.</margin>In the first gems of this plate, 103 and 104, is depicted that famous Canopus, held to be the most powerful of the gods, inasmuch as he extinguished Fire—the god of the Persians—with the water of the Nile, which he contained in a vessel surrounding himself. <margin>Kircher, final chapter of the Egyptian Forerunner.</margin>The Egyptians afterwards, according to their custom of rushing headlong into every kind of superstition, placed him among their sacred symbols. It is said that Antiochus, when about to fight against the Galatians, won a signal victory by the aid of this symbol placed upon his banners, after he had been warned in a dream to do so.

<margin>GEM 106.</margin>There follows on Plate XXVI Serapis, the well-known god of the Egyptians, whom that nation, devoted to superstitions, worshiped above others. # COMMENTARY. CHAPTER XV.

The same is said by Diodorus <note>Diodorus Siculus, a Greek historian</note> to be *at once Dionysus, Pluto,* AMMON, *and* JUPITER. Whose crown, says Macrobius, <margin>Book 1 of the Saturnalia.</margin> is distinguished by a BASKET <note>original: "calatho"; a tall, wicker basket-shaped headdress associated with fertility and the underworld</note>, which *both demonstrates the height of the* STAR, *and shows the power of its capacity: because all earthly things return into it, while they are snatched up by the heat sent into them.* From these points and another oracle of Apollo, he shows it to be apparent that the *nature of* SERAPIS and the SUN *is one and indivisible.* To these he adds that ISIS *is celebrated with every religious rite, who is either the* EARTH *or the* NATURE OF THINGS *lying beneath the* SUN. Whence common honors were held for both, engraved Gems, altars, and statues erected. Pausa- <margin>GEM 112.</margin> nias in his *Description of Corinth*: *From where one ascends from the sea to the higher parts of the shore, there is a temple of the* SUN, *and a grove of the Graces. A shrine erected to* SERAPIS *and* ISIS *is surrounded by a wall made of very large and selected stones.* The same author in his *Description of Laconia*: *The fragments of* SERAPIS and ISIS *are no more than seven stadia* <note>A stadium was an ancient Greek unit of distance, roughly 600 feet</note> *distant from Boeae.* And in his *Description of Messenia*: *Not far from the theater, there is a shrine of* SERAPIS and ISIS. Finally, in his *Description of Achaia*: *There are also in the same sanctuary [of Apollo] several standing statues of Aesculapius, and separately those of* SERAPIS and ISIS, *and all are made of Pentelic marble.* Saint Augustine also bears witness: *In almost all temples where* <margin>Book 18 of The City of God.</margin> ISIS and SERAPIS *were worshipped, there was also an* IMAGE, *which seemed to warn with a finger pressed to the lips, that* SILENCE *should be observed.* Hence, between both deities, one can see Harpocrates <note>The god of silence and secrets</note> in the middle on Gems 113 and 114. <margin>GEM 113. 114. GEM 115. 116. &amp; 117.</margin>

The last three, 115, 116, and 117, represent Mercury, worshipped by the Egyptians and Romans as the spirit of the universe, and the sole cause of causes. A ram stands by him, because *he, above all others, was thought to protect and increase the* FLOCKS.

But the fellowship of these Egyptian deities with the Romans, <margin>Pausanias in his Description of Corinth. GEM 105.</margin> noted by Prudentius, is beautifully shown by Gem 105, in which the goddess Juno is veiled, and just as she appears on coins, [holding] a spear

...holding a spear and a shallow ritual bowl, with a Gnostic inscription on the reverse: <term>SAKAOE SOPHE ADONAI</term> <note>original: "CAKAΩE COΦH AAΩNAI"; likely a corruption of Hebrew terms meaning "The Wise Lord of Hosts"</note>. When the Senate had decreed that the temples of Isis and Serapis were to be destroyed, and none of the workmen dared to touch them, he laid aside his bordered toga, seized an axe, and struck the doors of that temple himself. But (such is the <margin>Tertullian in the *Apologeticus*.</margin> blindness of the human mind) the Romans later restored these same deities and conferred upon them the highest majesty. Even Saint Jerome complains that in his own age at Rome, the heresy of Basilides <note>A prominent 2nd-century Gnostic teacher from Alexandria</note> had been revived in Jovinian, who from a Roman monk <margin>Book 2, *Against Jovinian*.</margin> became his disciple and a servant of Epicurus: *It has been nearly four hundred years,* he says, *since the preaching of Christ has shone in the world, during which time innumerable heresies have torn His tunic. Almost every error proceeded from the Chaldean, Syrian, and Greek languages.* <term>BASILIDES</term>, *the master of luxury and most shameful embraces, has after so many years been transformed into* <term>JOVINIAN</term> *as if into Euphorbus* <note>A reference to Pythagoras's claim of reincarnation; Jerome suggests the soul of the ancient heretic has returned in a new body</note>, *so that the* <term>LATIN</term> <term>LANGUAGE</term> *might also have its own heresy.*
